FILE README.md
1:# Plain
2:
3:A lightweight Markdown reader and editor for Windows 11.
4:
5:Double-click a `.md` file and you are reading. Make a change, and the file stays yours, byte for byte. Your library is a plain folder on disk: no database, no account, no network.
6:
7:![Plain in reading mode](docs/screenshot-light.png)
8:
9:![Plain in rich mode, dark theme](docs/screenshot-rich-dark.png)
10:
11:## What it does
12:
13:- **Three views of one document.** `read` renders the text, `edit` shows the source with syntax highlighting, and `rich` shows the source with the markup hidden, the way Typora does. Switch with `Ctrl+/` or by clicking the mode bar.
14:- **All the Markdown you need.** Tables, task lists, footnotes, callouts like `> [!note]`, KaTeX math, Mermaid diagrams, syntax-highlighted code, `==highlights==`, `[[wikilinks]]`, and images from your folder.
15:- **A folder as a library.** A file tree, create, rename, delete to the Recycle Bin, and full-text search across the folder.
16:- **Quick open.** `Ctrl+K` finds files by name, lists recent ones, and runs commands.
17:- **Careful saving.** Atomic writes, encodings and line endings preserved, recovery drafts in case of a crash, and a warning when a file changes on disk while you have it open.
18:- **A quiet interface.** One monospace font, light and dark themes, no toolbars, no icons.
19:
20:## Installation
21:
22:1. Unpack `Plain-0.1.0-win-x64.zip` into any folder, for example `C:\Program Files\Plain` or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Plain`. It contains a single `plain.exe`; there is no installer.
23:2. Run `plain.exe`. On first launch, Windows SmartScreen shows a blue "Windows protected your PC" dialog because the program is not code-signed. Click **More info → Run anyway**. It will not ask again.
24:3. To open `.md` files in Plain with a double-click: right-click any `.md` file → **Open with → Choose another app → Plain → Always**. Plain appears in that list after its first launch, but it never makes itself the default on its own.
25:
26:Nothing else needs to be installed: no Node, no Rust, no separate WebView2 runtime. Everything Plain needs already ships with Windows 11. If you move the folder later, just run the exe from its new location.
27:
28:## Using it
29:
30:- **Open a file.** Double-click it in Explorer, drop it onto the window, or press `Ctrl+O`.
31:- **Open a folder.** Press `Ctrl+Alt+O` or drop a folder onto the window. The file tree appears on the left, and `Ctrl+Shift+F` searches the contents of every file in it.
32:- **Edit.** `Ctrl+/` switches between reading and editing. Format with the usual shortcuts (`Ctrl+B`, `Ctrl+I`, `Ctrl+Shift+K`…) or with the small panel that appears above selected text.
33:- **Save.** `Ctrl+S`. Until you save, a `•` shows in the title, and a recovery draft is already stored in a safe place: if the program or the computer shuts down, Plain offers to restore your text on the next launch.
34:- **Settings.** `Ctrl+,` opens theme, font size, column width, line numbers, and a few more. Changes apply immediately.
35:- **Everything else** lives in the `file · edit · view · help` menu and in the command palette, `Ctrl+Shift+P`. The full list of keyboard shortcuts is under `help → shortcuts`, or press `F1`.
36:
37:## Keyboard shortcuts
38:
39:The complete list is inside the app: `help → shortcuts` or `F1`. Shortcuts work in any keyboard layout, so `Ctrl+B` is bold even when the layout is Cyrillic.
40:
41:## Where your data lives
42:
43:In `%APPDATA%\Plain` (that is `C:\Users\<you>\AppData\Roaming\Plain`):
44:
45:| File | Contents |
46:|---|---|
47:| `settings.json` | settings; easier to change with `Ctrl+,` |
48:| `drafts\` | recovery drafts of unsaved edits |
49:| `state.json` | open files, reading positions, the last folder |
50:
51:Plain creates nothing inside your own folders.
52:
53:## How it treats your files
54:
55:- Bytes you did not touch do not change: list markers, indentation, trailing spaces, the byte order mark, the final newline.
56:- Line endings are kept as they were, CRLF or LF. If a file mixes both, saving picks the dominant style and says so in the status bar.
57:- The encoding is kept: UTF-8, UTF-8 with BOM, UTF-16. For a legacy cp1251 file, Plain offers to convert it to UTF-8 the first time you save.
58:- Writes are atomic: the text goes to a temporary file next to the original, which is then replaced in one step. An interrupted write cannot corrupt a document.
59:- If a file changes on disk while it is open: with no edits of your own, it is reloaded quietly; with edits, Plain shows a warning and never overwrites the other change.
60:
61:## Building from source
62:
63:You need Node.js 22, Rust (`rustup` with the `stable-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` toolchain), and Visual Studio Build Tools with the "Desktop development with C++" workload.
64:
65:```
66:npm install
67:npm run tauri dev     # development build with live reload
68:npm run pack          # release build, produces dist-win\Plain-0.1.0-win-x64.zip
69:```
70:
71:Checks: `npm run build`, `npx vitest run`, and `cargo test` inside `src-tauri`. The manual checklist used before a release is in `CHECKLIST.md`.
72:
73:Stack: Tauri 2, React, CodeMirror 6, unified (remark and rehype), Shiki, KaTeX, Mermaid. Rust handles the file system, folder watching, and search.

FILE src-tauri/src/assoc.rs
1:// Per-user file association for `.md` and `.markdown` (spec §13). Everything
2:// is written under HKCU: the app offers itself in "open with" and appears in
3:// Settings → Default apps, and it never makes itself the default.
4:
5:use std::io;
6:
7:use winreg::enums::{HKEY_CURRENT_USER, REG_NONE};
8:use winreg::{RegKey, RegValue};
9:
10:const PROGID: &str = "Plain.md";
11:const EXTENSIONS: [&str; 2] = [".md", ".markdown"];
12:const CLASSES: &str = r"Software\Classes";
13:const CAPABILITIES: &str = r"Software\Plain\Capabilities";
14:const REGISTERED: &str = r"Software\RegisteredApplications";
15:
16:fn exe_path() -> Result<String, String> {
17:    std::env::current_exe()
18:        .map(|path| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
19:        .map_err(|error| error.to_string())
20:}
21:
22:/// What Explorer runs for a double-clicked file.
23:fn open_command(exe: &str) -> String {
24:    format!("\"{exe}\" \"%1\"")
25:}
26:
27:fn write(exe: &str) -> io::Result<()> {
28:    let hkcu = RegKey::predef(HKEY_CURRENT_USER);
29:
30:    // The ProgID: the name Explorer shows and the command it runs.
31:    let (progid, _) = hkcu.create_subkey(format!(r"{CLASSES}\{PROGID}"))?;
32:    progid.set_value("", &"markdown")?;
33:    progid.set_value("FriendlyTypeName", &"markdown")?;
34:    let (icon, _) = progid.create_subkey("DefaultIcon")?;
35:    icon.set_value("", &format!("{exe},0"))?;
36:    let (command, _) = progid.create_subkey(r"shell\open\command")?;
37:    command.set_value("", &open_command(exe))?;
38:
39:    // The extensions only *offer* the ProgID. Which one wins stays the
40:    // user's choice: nothing is written to the `.md` default (spec §13).
41:    for extension in EXTENSIONS {
42:        let (progids, _) = hkcu.create_subkey(format!(r"{CLASSES}\{extension}\OpenWithProgids"))?;
43:        progids.set_raw_value(
44:            PROGID,
45:            &RegValue {
46:                bytes: Vec::new().into(),
47:                vtype: REG_NONE,
48:            },
49:        )?;
50:    }
51:
52:    // Capabilities are what put the app in Settings → Default apps.
53:    let (capabilities, _) = hkcu.create_subkey(CAPABILITIES)?;
54:    capabilities.set_value("ApplicationName", &"Plain")?;
55:    capabilities.set_value("ApplicationDescription", &"a markdown reader and editor")?;
56:    let (associations, _) = capabilities.create_subkey("FileAssociations")?;
57:    for extension in EXTENSIONS {
58:        associations.set_value(extension, &PROGID)?;
59:    }
60:    let (registered, _) = hkcu.create_subkey(REGISTERED)?;
61:    registered.set_value("Plain", &CAPABILITIES)?;
62:
63:    Ok(())
64:}
65:
66:/// Undoing a registration that is already gone is not an error.
67:fn ignore_missing(result: io::Result<()>) -> io::Result<()> {
68:    match result {
69:        Err(error) if error.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
70:        other => other,
71:    }
72:}
73:
74:fn erase() -> io::Result<()> {
75:    let hkcu = RegKey::predef(HKEY_CURRENT_USER);
76:
77:    ignore_missing(hkcu.delete_subkey_all(format!(r"{CLASSES}\{PROGID}")))?;
78:    for extension in EXTENSIONS {
79:        // Only our own value goes; the key belongs to every app that offers
80:        // to open the extension.
81:        if let Ok(progids) = hkcu.open_subkey_with_flags(
82:            format!(r"{CLASSES}\{extension}\OpenWithProgids"),
83:            winreg::enums::KEY_ALL_ACCESS,
84:        ) {
85:            ignore_missing(progids.delete_value(PROGID))?;
86:        }
87:    }
88:    ignore_missing(hkcu.delete_subkey_all(r"Software\Plain"))?;
89:    if let Ok(registered) =
90:        hkcu.open_subkey_with_flags(REGISTERED, winreg::enums::KEY_ALL_ACCESS)
91:    {
92:        ignore_missing(registered.delete_value("Plain"))?;
93:    }
94:    Ok(())
95:}
96:
97:/// Explorer caches associations; without this the change shows up on the
98:/// next logon.
99:fn notify_shell() {
100:    use windows::Win32::UI::Shell::{SHChangeNotify, SHCNE_ASSOCCHANGED, SHCNF_IDLIST};
101:    unsafe { SHChangeNotify(SHCNE_ASSOCCHANGED, SHCNF_IDLIST, None, None) };
102:}
103:
104:#[tauri::command]
105:pub fn register_file_association() -> Result<(), String> {
106:    write(&exe_path()?).map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
107:    notify_shell();
108:    Ok(())
109:}
110:
111:#[tauri::command]
112:pub fn unregister_file_association() -> Result<(), String> {
113:    erase().map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
114:    notify_shell();
115:    Ok(())
116:}
117:
118:/// What `about` reports. True as long as `.md` still offers our ProgID.
119:#[tauri::command]
120:pub fn file_association_registered() -> bool {
121:    RegKey::predef(HKEY_CURRENT_USER)
122:        .open_subkey(format!(r"{CLASSES}\.md\OpenWithProgids"))
123:        .and_then(|key| key.get_raw_value(PROGID))
124:        .is_ok()
125:}
126:
127:/// Runs at every release start, so the recorded exe path follows the folder
128:/// when it is moved. Cheap, idempotent, and silent when it fails — a broken
129:/// association is not a reason to refuse to open a file.
130:#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]
131:pub fn register_quietly() {
132:    if let Err(error) = register_file_association() {
133:        eprintln!("plain: could not register the .md association: {error}");
134:    }
135:}
136:
137:#[cfg(test)]
138:mod tests {
139:    use super::open_command;
140:
141:    /// Both quotes matter: Explorer passes paths with spaces.
142:    #[test]
143:    fn the_open_command_quotes_the_exe_and_the_argument() {
144:        assert_eq!(
145:            open_command(r"C:\Program Files\Plain\plain.exe"),
146:            "\"C:\\Program Files\\Plain\\plain.exe\" \"%1\""
147:        );
148:    }
149:}

FILE scripts/pack.ps1
1:# Builds the release exe and drops it in a zip (spec S13: a folder with an
2:# exe, no installer). Run it as: npm run pack
3:
4:$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
5:$root = Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot
6:
7:if (-not (Get-Command cargo -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
8:    throw "cargo is not on PATH - install rustup (stable-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc)"
9:}
10:
11:$conf = Get-Content (Join-Path $root "src-tauri\tauri.conf.json") -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json
12:$version = $conf.version
13:$name = "Plain-$version"
14:
15:Write-Host "building $name..."
16:Push-Location $root
17:try {
18:    & npx tauri build --no-bundle
19:    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "tauri build exited with $LASTEXITCODE" }
20:}
21:finally {
22:    Pop-Location
23:}
24:
25:$exe = Join-Path $root "src-tauri\target\release\plain.exe"
26:if (-not (Test-Path $exe)) { throw "no exe at $exe" }
27:
28:$out = Join-Path $root "dist-win"
29:$folder = Join-Path $out $name
30:$zip = Join-Path $out "$name-win-x64.zip"
31:if (Test-Path $folder) { Remove-Item $folder -Recurse -Force }
32:if (Test-Path $zip) { Remove-Item $zip -Force }
33:New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $folder | Out-Null
34:
35:Copy-Item $exe $folder
36:Copy-Item (Join-Path $root "README.md") $folder
37:
38:Compress-Archive -Path $folder -DestinationPath $zip
39:$exeMb = [math]::Round((Get-Item $exe).Length / 1MB, 1)
40:$zipMb = [math]::Round((Get-Item $zip).Length / 1MB, 1)
41:Write-Host "plain.exe $exeMb MB -> $zip ($zipMb MB)"

18:        "height": 720,
19:        "minWidth": 480,
20:        "minHeight": 400,
21:        "decorations": true,
22:        "resizable": true,
23:        "dragDropEnabled": true
24:      }

28:      "assetProtocol": {

4:import { invoke } from "@tauri-apps/api/core";
5:import { open, save } from "@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog";
6:import { mkdir } from "@tauri-apps/plugin-fs";
7:import { appDataDir, join } from "@tauri-apps/api/path";
8:import { getCurrentWindow } from "@tauri-apps/api/window";
9:import { flushActiveEditor } from "../editor";
10:import { isDirty } from "../editor/buffers";
11:import { emitRerender } from "../read/events";
12:import { render } from "../read/pipeline";
13:import { countWords } from "../read/words";
14:import { serialize } from "./eol";
15:import { inTauri } from "./env";
16:import { docFields, fsError, hashFile, readFile, writeTextAtomic } from "./fs";
17:import { basename, pathKey } from "./paths";
18:import { reloadFromDisk } from "./save";
19:import { SETTINGS_FILE, serializeSettings } from "./settings";
20:import { activeDoc, makeDoc, useStore, type Doc } from "./store";
21:
22:/**
23: * Reads each path into the open list; the first one becomes active.
24: * Returns whether anything ended up open.
25: */
26:export async function openPaths(paths: string[]): Promise<boolean> {
27:  const { activate, openDoc, showBanner } = useStore.getState();
28:  let opened = false;
29:
30:  for (const path of paths) {
31:    // The same spelling is already open: show it, and do not read the file
32:    // a second time.
33:    const known = useStore.getState().docs.find((d) => d.id === pathKey(path));
34:    if (known) {
35:      activate(known.id);
36:      opened = true;
37:      continue;
38:    }
39:    try {
40:      const info = await readFile(path);
41:      // Rust hands back the one spelling the disk uses. A short `KOTENO~1`
42:      // name, another case or the other separator is the same file, and one
43:      // file gets one buffer (spec §8).
44:      const id = pathKey(info.path);
45:      const existing = useStore.getState().docs.find((d) => d.id === id);
46:      if (existing) {
47:        activate(existing.id);
48:        opened = true;
49:        continue;
50:      }
51:      const fields = docFields(info);
52:      openDoc(makeDoc({ id, path: info.path, ...fields }));
53:      // Bytes the encoding could not read are showing as `�`; saying so
54:      // now is what makes the question at save time make sense (spec §8).
55:      if (fields.decodeErrors) useStore.getState().setNote("decoded with errors");
56:      opened = true;
57:    } catch (error) {
58:      const failure = fsError(error);
59:      // A `recent` entry that points at nothing is worth less than the row
60:      // it takes up, and it will fail again next time it is clicked.
61:      if (isGone(failure)) useStore.getState().forgetRecent(path);
62:      showBanner({
63:        id: "open-failed",
64:        text: `couldn't open ${basename(path)} — ${failure.message}`,
65:        actions: [{ label: "dismiss", run: () => useStore.getState().dismissBanner("open-failed") }],
66:      });
67:    }
68:  }
69:  // The complaint was about a file that is no longer the one on screen.
70:  if (opened) useStore.getState().dismissBanner("open-failed");
71:  return opened;
72:}
73:
74:/**
75: * The file is not there. Rust hands back the OS message, and on Windows a
76: * missing file is error 2 and a missing folder on the way is error 3.
77: */
78:function isGone(failure: { kind: string; message: string }): boolean {
79:  return failure.kind === "missing" || /\(os error [23]\)/.test(failure.message);
80:}
81:
82:/**
83: * `Ctrl+N`. With a library open the tree grows a row with an inline name
84: * field in the root folder; without one it is a nameless buffer whose
85: * `Ctrl+S` is Save As (spec §6).
86: */
87:let untitled = 0;
88:
89:export function newDoc(): void {
90:  const store = useStore.getState();
91:  if (store.libraryPath) {
92:    store.setRailCollapsed(false);
93:    store.setRailView("files");
94:    store.setTreeDraft({ parent: store.libraryPath, kind: "file" });
95:    return;
96:  }
97:  // Unique across runs as well as within one: a restored draft keeps the id
98:  // it was written under, and two sessions must not collide on it (spec §8).
99:  untitled += 1;
100:  store.openDoc(
101:    makeDoc({
102:      id: `untitled-${Date.now().toString(36)}-${untitled}`,
103:      path: null,
104:      text: "",
105:      mode: "edit",
106:      eol: store.settings.files.newFileEol,
107:    }),
108:  );
109:}
110:
111:/** `F5`: build the document again, and re-read it when it is clean. */
112:export function refresh(): void {
113:  // Typing and hitting F5 with no pause in between must not read as clean:
114:  // the store lags the editor until its idle callback (spec §8).
115:  flushActiveEditor();
116:  const doc = activeDoc(useStore.getState());
117:  if (!doc?.path) {
118:    emitRerender();
119:    return;
120:  }
121:  if (isDirty(doc.id, doc.text)) {
122:    useStore.getState().setMessage("unsaved changes — save or reload from banner");
123:    emitRerender();
124:    return;
125:  }
126:  void reloadFromDisk(doc.id, { note: "" }).then(emitRerender);
127:}

129:/**
130: * Takes whatever Rust parked for us — launch arguments, or the argv of a
131: * second launch. Opening a file this way collapses the rail (spec §6).
132: */
133:export async function pendingPaths(): Promise<string[]> {
134:  return inTauri ? invoke<string[]>("take_pending_paths") : [];
135:}
136:
137:/** Folder arguments, parked separately: a folder is a library (spec §6). */
138:export async function pendingFolders(): Promise<string[]> {
139:  return inTauri ? invoke<string[]>("take_pending_folders") : [];
140:}
141:
142:export async function drainPendingPaths(): Promise<void> {
143:  const folders = await pendingFolders();
144:  const first = folders[0];
145:  if (first) await openLibraryPath(first);
146:  const paths = await pendingPaths();
147:  if (paths.length === 0) return;
148:  // A file on its own arrives with the rail out of the way; a file that came
149:  // with its folder does not, because the folder is the point.
150:  if ((await openPaths(paths)) && !first) useStore.getState().setRailCollapsed(true);
151:}
152:
153:export async function openFile(): Promise<void> {
154:  if (!inTauri) return;
155:  const extensions = useStore
156:    .getState()
157:    .settings.library.extensions.map((e) => e.replace(/^\./, ""));
158:  const picked = await open({
159:    multiple: false,
160:    directory: false,
161:    filters: [{ name: "markdown", extensions }],
162:  });
163:  if (typeof picked === "string") await openPaths([picked]);
164:}
165:
166:export async function openLibrary(): Promise<void> {
167:  if (!inTauri) return;
168:  // recursive: the fs scope has to reach files in sub-folders, not just the root.
169:  const picked = await open({ directory: true, multiple: false, recursive: true });
170:  if (typeof picked !== "string") return;
171:  await openLibraryPath(picked);
172:}
173:
174:/**
175: * The one way a folder becomes the library: the dialog, a dropped folder, a
176: * folder given as an argument and the session all come through here. The
177: * tree, the watcher and the asset scope follow the store (spec §6, §9).
178: */
179:export async function openLibraryPath(path: string, announce = true): Promise<void> {
180:  const store = useStore.getState();
181:  if (inTauri) {
182:    // Images and links inside the library have to be reachable (spec §9).
183:    await invoke("allow_asset_dir", { path }).catch(() => undefined);
184:  }
185:  store.setLibraryPath(path);
186:  // A folder that is opened afresh starts with everything unfolded; the
187:  // session puts its own set back after this (bootstrap.ts).
188:  if (announce) store.setCollapsed([]);
189:  store.setRailView("files");
190:  store.setRailCollapsed(false);
191:  if (announce) store.setMessage(`library · ${basename(path)}`);
192:}

194:export async function toggleFullscreen(): Promise<void> {
195:  if (!inTauri) return;
196:  const win = getCurrentWindow();
197:  await win.setFullscreen(!(await win.isFullscreen()));
198:}

200:export async function toggleAlwaysOnTop(): Promise<void> {
201:  const store = useStore.getState();
202:  const next = !store.alwaysOnTop;
203:  if (inTauri) await getCurrentWindow().setAlwaysOnTop(next);
204:  store.setAlwaysOnTop(next);
205:  store.setMessage(next ? "always on top" : "always on top off");
206:}

208:/**
209: * Same as `openPaths`, but the document that was active stays active — this
210: * is what `Ctrl+click` on a link in read does (spec §5.1).
211: */
212:export async function openPathsInBackground(paths: string[]): Promise<boolean> {
213:  const before = useStore.getState().activeId;
214:  const opened = await openPaths(paths);
215:  if (before) useStore.getState().activate(before);
216:  return opened;
217:}

266:/**
267: * `Ctrl+,` until the settings screen lands. The file is created with the
268: * defaults so there is something to edit (spec §10).
269: */
270:export async function openSettingsFile(): Promise<void> {
271:  if (!inTauri) return;
272:  const dir = await appDataDir();
273:  const file = await join(dir, SETTINGS_FILE);
274:  try {
275:    await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true }).catch(() => undefined);
276:    if ((await hashFile(file)) === null) {
277:      // What is in force right now, not the defaults: the settings screen
278:      // may already have moved things the file never got to hold.
279:      await writeTextAtomic(file, `${serializeSettings(useStore.getState().settings)}\n`);
280:    }
281:    await openPaths([file]);
282:    useStore.getState().setMode("edit");
283:  } catch (error) {
284:    useStore.getState().setMessage(`couldn't open settings — ${fsError(error).message}`);
285:  }

294:/**
295: * `help → about` (spec §13): the version, the association and the two links.
296: * A release registers itself at every start, so the entry here only has to
297: * offer the way back out.
298: */
299:export async function showAbout(): Promise<void> {
300:  const store = useStore.getState();
301:  const go = async (url: string) => {
302:    if (!inTauri) return;
303:    const { openUrl } = await import("@tauri-apps/plugin-opener");
304:    await openUrl(url).catch(() => store.setMessage("couldn't open the link"));
305:  };
306:  const toggle = async (registered: boolean) => {
307:    const command = registered ? "unregister_file_association" : "register_file_association";
308:    try {
309:      await invoke(command);
310:      store.setMessage(registered ? "unregistered" : "registered for .md");
311:    } catch (error) {
312:      store.setMessage(`couldn't change the association — ${String(error)}`);
313:    }
314:    await showAbout();
315:  };
316:
317:  const registered = await isRegistered();
318:  store.setDialog({
319:    title: "plain 0.1.0",
320:    lines: [
321:      "a markdown reader and editor that leaves your files alone.",
322:      registered ? "registered for .md · .markdown" : "not registered for .md",
323:    ],
324:    actions: [
325:      { label: "default apps", run: () => void go("ms-settings:defaultapps") },
326:      { label: "github", run: () => void go("https://github.com/gregmos/plain") },
327:      { label: registered ? "unregister" : "register", run: () => void toggle(registered) },
328:      { label: "close", run: () => store.setDialog(null) },
329:    ],
330:    cancel: () => store.setDialog(null),
331:  });
332:}

340:/* -------------------------------------------------- plain text (wave 5b) */
341:
342:/** Chrome the reader sees but a paste should not carry. */
343:const DROP_CLASSES = ["code-bar", "h-anchor", "img-holder"];
344:
345:/** A blank line around them, the way a paragraph reads. */
346:const PARAGRAPH_TAGS = new Set([
347:  "p", "div", "h1", "h2", "h3", "h4", "h5", "h6",
348:  "pre", "blockquote", "section", "article", "figure", "table", "hr", "details",
349:]);
350:
351:/** One line each — a list is a list, not a stack of paragraphs. */
352:const LINE_TAGS = new Set([
353:  "li", "ul", "ol", "tr", "dl", "dt", "dd", "figcaption", "summary",
354:]);
355:
356:const ENTITIES: Record<string, string> = {
357:  amp: "&", lt: "<", gt: ">", quot: '"', apos: "'", nbsp: " ", hellip: "…", mdash: "—", ndash: "–",
358:};
359:
360:function decode(text: string): string {
361:  return text.replace(/&(#x?[0-9a-f]+|[a-z]+);/gi, (whole, body: string) => {
362:    if (body.startsWith("#")) {
363:      const code = body[1] === "x" || body[1] === "X"
364:        ? parseInt(body.slice(2), 16)
365:        : parseInt(body.slice(1), 10);
366:      return Number.isFinite(code) ? String.fromCodePoint(code) : whole;
367:    }
368:    return ENTITIES[body.toLowerCase()] ?? whole;
369:  });
370:}
371:
372:function attribute(tag: string, name: string): string | null {
373:  const m = new RegExp(`\\s${name}\\s*=\\s*("([^"]*)"|'([^']*)')`, "i").exec(tag);
374:  return m ? decode(m[2] ?? m[3] ?? "") : null;
375:}
376:
377:/**
378: * The rendered HTML as text a mail client would accept: no `#`, no `*`, one
379: * list item per line. Done on the string rather than through a DOM, so it
380: * needs no layout, no document, and runs the same in a test as in the app.
381: */
382:export function htmlToPlainText(html: string): string {
383:  let out = "";
384:  let pre = 0;
385:  let i = 0;
386:
387:  const push = (text: string) => {
388:    // A collapsed space right after a line break is the markup's, not the
389:    // author's — except inside `pre`, where every space is the author's.
390:    out += pre === 0 && out.endsWith("\n") ? text.replace(/^[ \t]+/, "") : text;
391:  };
392:  /** At least `n` line breaks here — never more than are wanted. */
393:  const brk = (n: number) => {
394:    if (out === "") return;
395:    out = out.replace(/[ \t]+$/, "");
396:    const have = /\n*$/.exec(out)?.[0].length ?? 0;
397:    out += "\n".repeat(Math.max(0, n - have));
398:  };
399:
400:  while (i < html.length) {
401:    const lt = html.indexOf("<", i);
402:    if (lt < 0) {
403:      push(pre > 0 ? decode(html.slice(i)) : decode(html.slice(i)).replace(/\s+/g, " "));
404:      break;
405:    }
406:    if (lt > i) {
407:      const text = decode(html.slice(i, lt));
408:      push(pre > 0 ? text : text.replace(/\s+/g, " "));
409:    }
410:    const gt = html.indexOf(">", lt);
411:    if (gt < 0) break;
412:
413:    const tag = html.slice(lt, gt + 1);
414:    const name = /^<\/?\s*([a-z0-9]+)/i.exec(tag)?.[1]?.toLowerCase() ?? "";
415:    const closing = tag[1] === "/";
416:
417:    // A comment or a doctype carries nothing.
418:    if (tag.startsWith("<!")) {
419:      i = gt + 1;
420:      continue;
421:    }
422:
423:    const classes = closing ? null : attribute(tag, "class");
424:    if (classes && DROP_CLASSES.some((c) => classes.split(/\s+/).includes(c))) {
425:      i = skipElement(html, gt + 1, name);
426:      continue;
427:    }
428:
429:    if (name === "img") push(attribute(tag, "alt") ?? "");
430:    else if (name === "br") brk(1);
431:    else if (name === "pre") pre += closing ? -1 : 1;
432:
433:    if (PARAGRAPH_TAGS.has(name)) brk(2);
434:    else if (LINE_TAGS.has(name)) brk(1);
435:    i = gt + 1;
436:  }
437:
438:  return out
439:    .replace(/[ \t]+\n/g, "\n")
440:    .replace(/\n{3,}/g, "\n\n")
441:    .trim();
442:}
443:
444:/** Position just past the matching close tag of an element already opened. */
445:function skipElement(html: string, from: number, name: string): number {
446:  let depth = 1;
447:  let i = from;
448:  const tags = new RegExp(`<(/?)${name}\\b[^>]*>`, "gi");
449:  tags.lastIndex = from;
450:  for (let m = tags.exec(html); m; m = tags.exec(html)) {
451:    depth += m[1] ? -1 : 1;
452:    i = tags.lastIndex;
453:    if (depth === 0) return i;
454:  }
455:  return html.length;
456:}
457:
458:/**
459: * The document as it looks, not as it is written. Null when it is too big to
460: * render at all (spec §8).
461: */
462:export function plainTextOf(doc: Doc): string | null {
463:  if (doc.large) return null;
464:  return htmlToPlainText(render(doc.text).html);
465:}
466:
467:/** `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C`: the document as plain text, on the clipboard. */
468:export async function copyPlainText(): Promise<void> {
469:  const store = useStore.getState();
470:  flushActiveEditor();
471:  const doc = activeDoc(useStore.getState());
472:  if (!doc) return;
473:
474:  const text = plainTextOf(doc);
475:  if (text === null) {
476:    store.setMessage("too large to render");
477:    return;
478:  }
479:  try {
480:    await navigator.clipboard.writeText(text);
481:    store.setMessage(`copied as plain text · ${countWords(text).toLocaleString("en-US")} words`);
482:  } catch {
483:    store.setMessage("couldn't copy");
484:  }
485:}
486:
487:/** `file → export as text…`: the same text, written as a `.txt`. */
488:export async function exportPlainText(): Promise<void> {
489:  if (!inTauri) return;
490:  const store = useStore.getState();
491:  flushActiveEditor();
492:  const doc = activeDoc(useStore.getState());
493:  if (!doc) return;
494:
495:  const text = plainTextOf(doc);
496:  if (text === null) {
497:    store.setMessage("too large to render");
498:    return;
499:  }
500:
501:  const name = doc.path ? basename(doc.path) : doc.title;
502:  const picked = await save({
503:    defaultPath: `${name.replace(/\.[^.]+$/, "")}.txt`,
504:    filters: [{ name: "text", extensions: ["txt"] }],
505:  });
506:  if (typeof picked !== "string") return;
507:
508:  try {
509:    // UTF-8, no BOM; the line endings new files get (spec §10).
510:    await writeTextAtomic(picked, serialize(text, store.settings.files.newFileEol));
511:    store.setMessage(`exported ${basename(picked)}`);
512:  } catch (error) {
513:    store.setMessage(`couldn't export — ${fsError(error).message}`);
514:  }
515:}
516:
517:/* ------------------------------------------------------- pdf (spec §4) */
518:
519:/**
520: * `Ctrl+P`. Printing prints what is on screen, so the document has to be in
521: * read first; the mode goes back after the dialog closes.
522: */
523:export async function exportPdf(): Promise<void> {
524:  const store = useStore.getState();
525:  const doc = activeDoc(store);
526:  if (!doc) return;
527:
528:  const was = doc.mode;
529:  if (was !== "read") {
530:    flushActiveEditor();
531:    store.setMode("read");
532:    // The read view builds the document on entry; printing before it is
533:    // ready would print an empty page.
534:    await new Promise((done) => setTimeout(done, 400));
535:  }
536:
537:  const restore = () => {
538:    window.removeEventListener("afterprint", restore);
539:    if (was !== "read") useStore.getState().setMode(was);
540:  };
541:  window.addEventListener("afterprint", restore);
542:
543:  try {
544:    window.print();
545:  } catch {
546:    store.setMessage("couldn't open the print dialog");
547:    restore();
548:  }
549:}

FILE src/app/keys.ts
1:// One keyboard layer for the whole app. There are no chords here: every
2:// binding comes from the command registry (spec §12), so a shortcut and the
3:// menu entry that shows it can never drift apart. Tree keys (`F2`, `Delete`,
4:// the arrows) are local to the tree and live in the rail.
5:
6:import { tinykeys } from "tinykeys";
7:import { chordToPattern, matchesChord } from "./chords";
8:import { commands } from "./registry";
9:
10:/**
11: * WebView2 keeps its own accelerators (spec §12 leaves them on for zoom), but
12: * these must not reach it — they are ours, or they would reload or print.
13: */
14:const SWALLOW = [
15:  "Ctrl+F",
16:  "Ctrl+P",
17:  "Ctrl+G",
18:  "F5",
19:  "Ctrl+S",
20:  "Ctrl+Shift+S",
21:  "Ctrl+N",
22:  "Ctrl+O",
23:  "Ctrl+H",
24:  "Ctrl+K",
25:  "F1",
26:];
27:
28:/** chord -> action, straight out of the registry. */
29:export function bindings(): Record<string, () => void> {
30:  const map: Record<string, () => void> = {};
31:  for (const command of commands) {
32:    if (!command.chord) continue;
33:    map[command.chord] = () => void command.run();
34:  }
35:  return map;
36:}
37:
38:export function installKeys(): () => void {
39:  const swallow = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
40:    if (SWALLOW.some((chord) => matchesChord(chord, event))) event.preventDefault();
41:  };
42:  window.addEventListener("keydown", swallow, { capture: true });
43:
44:  // WebView2 would otherwise show its own context menu (spec §12). Bubble
45:  // phase, so an element that has its own menu can stop this first.
46:  const noMenu = (event: MouseEvent) => event.preventDefault();
47:  window.addEventListener("contextmenu", noMenu);
48:
49:  const map: Record<string, (event: KeyboardEvent) => void> = {};
50:  for (const [chord, run] of Object.entries(bindings())) {
51:    map[chordToPattern(chord)] = (event) => {
52:      event.preventDefault();
53:      run();
54:    };
55:  }
56:
57:  const off = tinykeys(window, map, {
58:    // Shortcuts must keep working while the editor (contenteditable) has
59:    // focus, so only auto-repeat and IME composition are skipped.
60:    ignore: (event) => event.repeat || event.isComposing,
61:  });
62:
63:  return () => {
64:    window.removeEventListener("keydown", swallow, { capture: true });
65:    window.removeEventListener("contextmenu", noMenu);
66:    off();
67:  };
68:}

FILE src/editor/keymap.ts
1:// CodeMirror matches key bindings by `key`, which breaks every letter chord
2:// in a Russian layout (spec §12). Our commands go through this thin layer
3:// instead: same chord spelling as the app, matched by KeyboardEvent.code.
4:
5:import {
6:  deleteLine,
7:  indentLess,
8:  indentMore,
9:  redo,
10:  selectAll,
11:  undo,
12:} from "@codemirror/commands";
13:import { foldCode, unfoldCode } from "@codemirror/language";
14:import { selectNextOccurrence } from "@codemirror/search";
15:import type { Extension } from "@codemirror/state";
16:import { ViewPlugin, type Command } from "@codemirror/view";
17:import { matchesChord, parseChord } from "../app/chords";
18:import {
19:  cycleList,
20:  insertLink,
21:  tabIndent,
22:  toggleBold,
23:  toggleCheckbox,
24:  toggleCodeBlock,
25:  toggleHeading,
26:  toggleInlineCode,
27:  toggleItalic,
28:  toggleQuote,
29:} from "./commands";
30:
31:export interface CodeBinding {
32:  chord: string;
33:  run: Command;
34:}
35:
36:/**
37: * Runs the first binding whose physical chord matches. This listens on the
38: * editor in the capture phase rather than through CodeMirror's own keydown
39: * handling, because the app swallows Ctrl+F/Ctrl+G with preventDefault on
40: * window (spec §12) and CodeMirror skips its handlers once that is set.
41: */
42:export function codeKeymap(bindings: CodeBinding[]): Extension {
43:  const compiled = bindings.map((b) => ({ chord: parseChord(b.chord), run: b.run }));
44:  return ViewPlugin.define((view) => {
45:    const onKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
46:      if (event.isComposing) return;
47:      // The find panel has its own keys.
48:      if ((event.target as HTMLElement | null)?.closest(".cm-panels")) return;
49:      for (const binding of compiled) {
50:        if (!matchesChord(binding.chord, event)) continue;
51:        if (binding.run(view)) event.preventDefault();
52:        return;
53:      }
54:    };
55:    view.dom.addEventListener("keydown", onKeydown, true);
56:    return { destroy: () => view.dom.removeEventListener("keydown", onKeydown, true) };
57:  });
58:}

FILE src/editor/remote.ts
1:// Find, replace and `go to line` are app commands (spec §12), so their chords
2:// live in the registry and the editor only answers the events. Without this
3:// the same chord would be handled twice: once by CodeMirror, once by the app.
4:
5:import { findNext, findPrevious, gotoLine, openSearchPanel } from "@codemirror/search";
6:import type { Extension } from "@codemirror/state";
7:import { ViewPlugin } from "@codemirror/view";
8:import { FIND, FIND_STEP, GOTO_LINE_PROMPT, REPLACE } from "../read/events";
9:import { openReplace } from "./findPanel";
10:
11:export const remoteCommands: Extension = ViewPlugin.define((view) => {
12:  const find = () => openSearchPanel(view);
13:  const replace = () => openReplace(view);
14:  const step = (event: Event) => {
15:    const direction = (event as CustomEvent<1 | -1>).detail;
16:    if (direction < 0) findPrevious(view);
17:    else findNext(view);
18:  };
19:  const goto = () => gotoLine(view);
20:
21:  window.addEventListener(FIND, find);
22:  window.addEventListener(REPLACE, replace);
23:  window.addEventListener(FIND_STEP, step);
24:  window.addEventListener(GOTO_LINE_PROMPT, goto);
25:
26:  return {
27:    destroy() {
28:      window.removeEventListener(FIND, find);
29:      window.removeEventListener(REPLACE, replace);
30:      window.removeEventListener(FIND_STEP, step);
31:      window.removeEventListener(GOTO_LINE_PROMPT, goto);
32:    },
33:  };
34:});

codex
В сохранении проверяю оставшиеся окна между `await`, закрытием документа и Save As. В rich уже видны подозрительные случаи с многострочными ссылками и regex внутри inline code; сверю их с фактическим поведением CodeMirror.

1:// Everything that touches the user's files (spec §8). A read reports the
2:// encoding, the BOM and the line endings so a later save can put the bytes
3:// back the way they were; a write goes to a temp file in the same folder and

19:/// The three cases the front end tells apart: `conflict` stops the save and
20:/// raises a banner, `missing` may be recreated on purpose, `io` is reported.
21:#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]
22:pub struct FsError {
23:    kind: &'static str,
24:    message: String,
25:}
26:
27:impl FsError {
28:    fn io(message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
29:        Self {
30:            kind: "io",
31:            message: message.into(),
32:        }
33:    }
34:
35:    fn conflict() -> Self {
36:        Self {
37:            kind: "conflict",
38:            message: "file changed on disk".into(),
39:        }
40:    }
41:
42:    fn missing(path: &Path) -> Self {
43:        Self {
44:            kind: "missing",
45:            message: format!("{} is gone", path.display()),
46:        }
47:    }
48:}
49:
50:impl From<std::io::Error> for FsError {
51:    fn from(error: std::io::Error) -> Self {
52:        Self::io(error.to_string())
53:    }
54:}

56:pub type FsResult<T> = Result<T, FsError>;

60:#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]
61:#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
62:pub struct FileInfo {
63:    /// The one spelling of this file — see `canonical`. The front end keys
64:    /// its documents off it, so `C:\Users\KOTENO~1\a.md` and the long name
65:    /// behind it never end up as two buffers over one file.
66:    path: String,
67:    text: String,
68:    /// encoding_rs label, lowercase: `utf-8`, `utf-16le`, `windows-1251`…
69:    encoding: String,
70:    bom: bool,
71:    /// What the file has: `crlf` `lf` `cr` `mixed` `none`.
72:    eol: String,
73:    /// What a save writes back; `crlf` when the file has no line break yet.
74:    dominant_eol: String,
75:    final_newline: bool,
76:    hash: String,
77:    mtime_ms: f64,
78:    size: u64,
79:    read_only: bool,
80:    /// Bytes that the chosen encoding could not decode became replacement
81:    /// characters. Saving over the original would make that permanent, so
82:    /// the front end asks first (spec §8).
83:    decode_errors: bool,
84:}
85:
86:/// A byte-order mark and how many bytes of it to skip.
87:fn bom_of(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<(&'static Encoding, usize)> {
88:    if bytes.starts_with(&[0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF]) {
89:        Some((UTF_8, 3))
90:    } else if bytes.starts_with(&[0xFF, 0xFE]) {
91:        Some((UTF_16LE, 2))
92:    } else if bytes.starts_with(&[0xFE, 0xFF]) {
93:        Some((UTF_16BE, 2))
94:    } else {
95:        None
96:    }
97:}
98:
99:/// BOM, then valid UTF-8, then chardetng (spec §8). `forced` comes from
100:/// `reopen as…` and wins over all of it.
101:fn detect(bytes: &[u8], forced: Option<&str>) -> (&'static Encoding, usize) {
102:    if let Some(label) = forced {
103:        let encoding = Encoding::for_label(label.as_bytes()).unwrap_or(UTF_8);
104:        let skip = match bom_of(bytes) {
105:            Some((found, len)) if found == encoding => len,
106:            _ => 0,
107:        };
108:        return (encoding, skip);
109:    }
110:    if let Some(found) = bom_of(bytes) {
111:        return found;
112:    }
113:    if std::str::from_utf8(bytes).is_ok() {
114:        return (UTF_8, 0);
115:    }
116:    // UTF-8 was already ruled out above, so the detector only has to choose
117:    // between the legacy encodings.
118:    let mut detector = EncodingDetector::new(Iso2022JpDetection::Deny);
119:    detector.feed(bytes, true);
120:    (detector.guess(None, Utf8Detection::Deny), 0)
121:}
122:
123:/// Bytes to text through exactly the detection an open uses (spec §8), for
124:/// callers that want nothing else from the file — the folder search, which
125:/// would otherwise never find a word in a cp1251 or UTF-16 document.
126:pub fn decode_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> String {
127:    let (encoding, skip) = detect(bytes, None);
128:    encoding
129:        .decode_without_bom_handling(&bytes[skip.min(bytes.len())..])
130:        .0
131:        .into_owned()
132:}
133:
134:/// `(what the file has, what a save writes back)`.
135:fn line_endings(text: &str) -> (&'static str, &'static str) {
136:    let (mut crlf, mut lf, mut cr) = (0usize, 0usize, 0usize);
137:    let bytes = text.as_bytes();
138:    let mut index = 0;
139:    while index < bytes.len() {
140:        match bytes[index] {
141:            b'\r' => {
142:                if bytes.get(index + 1) == Some(&b'\n') {
143:                    crlf += 1;
144:                    index += 1;
145:                } else {
146:                    cr += 1;
147:                }
148:            }
149:            b'\n' => lf += 1,
150:            _ => {}
151:        }
152:        index += 1;
153:    }
154:
155:    let kinds = u8::from(crlf > 0) + u8::from(lf > 0) + u8::from(cr > 0);
156:    let dominant = if crlf > 0 && crlf >= lf && crlf >= cr {
157:        "crlf"
158:    } else if lf > 0 && lf >= cr {
159:        "lf"
160:    } else if cr > 0 {
161:        "cr"
162:    } else {
163:        // Nothing to go by: the Windows default (§8, files.newFileEol).
164:        "crlf"
165:    };
166:    let eol = match kinds {
167:        0 => "none",
168:        1 => dominant,
169:        _ => "mixed",
170:    };
171:    (eol, dominant)
172:}
173:
174:/* ------------------------------------------------------ one path per file */
175:
176:/// `fs::canonicalize` hands back a verbatim path (`\\?\C:\…`) that no user
177:/// ever types and half of Windows will not take. This puts it back into the
178:/// ordinary form without giving up what canonicalizing found.
179:fn plain_path(path: PathBuf) -> PathBuf {
180:    let text = path.as_os_str().to_string_lossy();
181:    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix(r"\\?\UNC\") {
182:        return PathBuf::from(format!(r"\\{rest}"));
183:    }
184:    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix(r"\\?\") {
185:        // A drive letter is safe to unwrap; a device path is left alone.
186:        let mut start = rest.chars();
187:        if matches!((start.next(), start.next()), (Some(letter), Some(':')) if letter.is_ascii_alphabetic())
188:        {
189:            return PathBuf::from(rest);
190:        }
191:    }
192:    path
193:}
194:
195:/// The spelling the disk itself uses: 8.3 aliases expanded, links followed,
196:/// the real case. One file must have one identity or it gets two buffers
197:/// (spec §8). A file that does not exist yet — Save As onto a new name — is
198:/// resolved through its folder instead.
199:pub fn canonical(path: &Path) -> PathBuf {
200:    if let Ok(found) = fs::canonicalize(path) {
201:        return plain_path(found);
202:    }
203:    let (Some(parent), Some(name)) = (path.parent(), path.file_name()) else {
204:        return path.to_path_buf();
205:    };
206:    if parent.as_os_str().is_empty() {
207:        return path.to_path_buf();
208:    }
209:    match fs::canonicalize(parent) {
210:        Ok(folder) => plain_path(folder).join(name),
211:        Err(_) => path.to_path_buf(),
212:    }
213:}
214:
215:/// For the paths that do not come back through `read_file`: the Save As
216:/// dialog, and anything else that hands us a name the user typed.
217:#[tauri::command]
218:pub fn canonical_path(path: String) -> String {
219:    canonical(Path::new(&path)).to_string_lossy().into_owned()
220:}
221:
222:fn mtime_ms(meta: &fs::Metadata) -> f64 {
223:    meta.modified()
224:        .ok()
225:        .and_then(|time| time.duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH).ok())
226:        .map(|since| since.as_millis() as f64)
227:        .unwrap_or(0.0)
228:}
229:
230:/// The text comes back exactly as it is on disk, CRLF and all: CodeMirror
231:/// normalizes it, and the front end keeps `eol` to put it back.
232:pub fn read_info(path: &Path, forced: Option<&str>) -> FsResult<FileInfo> {
233:    let bytes = fs::read(path)?;
234:    let meta = fs::metadata(path)?;
235:    let (encoding, skip) = detect(&bytes, forced);
236:    let (text, decode_errors) = encoding.decode_without_bom_handling(&bytes[skip..]);
237:    let (eol, dominant) = line_endings(&text);
238:    Ok(FileInfo {
239:        path: canonical(path).to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
240:        decode_errors,
241:        final_newline: text.ends_with('\n') || text.ends_with('\r'),
242:        eol: eol.into(),
243:        dominant_eol: dominant.into(),
244:        encoding: encoding.name().to_ascii_lowercase(),
245:        bom: skip > 0,
246:        hash: blake3::hash(&bytes).to_hex().to_string(),
247:        mtime_ms: mtime_ms(&meta),
248:        size: meta.len(),
249:        read_only: meta.permissions().readonly(),
250:        text: text.into_owned(),
251:    })
252:}
253:
254:#[tauri::command]
255:pub fn read_file(path: String, encoding: Option<String>) -> FsResult<FileInfo> {
256:    read_info(Path::new(&path), encoding.as_deref())
257:}
258:
259:/// `None` means the file is not there. Access denied or a network hiccup is
260:/// an error, not a deletion — the front end must not say `deleted on disk`
261:/// because a read failed (spec §8).
262:#[tauri::command]
263:pub fn hash_file(path: String) -> FsResult<Option<String>> {
264:    match fs::read(&path) {
265:        Ok(bytes) => Ok(Some(blake3::hash(&bytes).to_hex().to_string())),
266:        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
267:        Err(error) => Err(FsError::io(error.to_string())),
268:    }
269:}
270:
271:/* --------------------------------------------------------------- writing */
272:
273:/// encoding_rs turns the two UTF-16 encodings into UTF-8 on the way out, so
274:/// they are done by hand. A legacy encoding that cannot hold the text is a
275:/// refusal: writing `&#1234;` in its place would corrupt the file (spec §8).
276:fn encode(text: &str, label: &str) -> FsResult<Vec<u8>> {
277:    match label {
278:        "utf-16le" => Ok(text.encode_utf16().flat_map(u16::to_le_bytes).collect()),
279:        "utf-16be" => Ok(text.encode_utf16().flat_map(u16::to_be_bytes).collect()),
280:        _ => {
281:            let encoding = Encoding::for_label(label.as_bytes()).unwrap_or(UTF_8);
282:            let (bytes, _, had_errors) = encoding.encode(text);
283:            if had_errors {
284:                return Err(FsError::io(format!(
285:                    "can't encode in {} — convert to utf-8",
286:                    encoding.name().to_ascii_lowercase()
287:                )));
288:            }
289:            Ok(bytes.into_owned())
290:        }
291:    }
292:}
293:
294:fn bom_bytes(label: &str) -> &'static [u8] {
295:    match label {
296:        "utf-16le" => &[0xFF, 0xFE],
297:        "utf-16be" => &[0xFE, 0xFF],
298:        _ => &[0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF],
299:    }
300:}
301:
302:/// The front end already did this; doing it again keeps a pasted `\n` out of
303:/// a CRLF file. `keep` is for our own files in %APPDATA%.
304:fn apply_eol(text: &str, eol: &str) -> String {
305:    if eol == "keep" {
306:        return text.to_string();
307:    }
308:    let lf = if text.contains('\r') {
309:        text.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace('\r', "\n")
310:    } else {
311:        text.to_string()
312:    };
313:    match eol {
314:        "crlf" => lf.replace('\n', "\r\n"),
315:        "cr" => lf.replace('\n', "\r"),
316:        _ => lf,
317:    }
318:}
319:
320:fn wide(path: &Path) -> Vec<u16> {
321:    path.as_os_str().encode_wide().chain(Some(0)).collect()
322:}
323:
324:/// Writes the bytes into a temp file next to the target and flushes them to
325:/// the platter (`sync_all` is FlushFileBuffers). Creates the folder, so it is
326:/// only ever called once the write is known to be allowed.
327:fn stage(dir: &Path, bytes: &[u8]) -> FsResult<PathBuf> {
328:    fs::create_dir_all(dir)?;
329:    let mut temp = tempfile::NamedTempFile::new_in(dir)?;
330:    temp.write_all(bytes)?;
331:    temp.flush()?;
332:    temp.as_file().sync_all()?;
333:    let (file, path) = temp.keep().map_err(|error| FsError::io(error.to_string()))?;
334:    drop(file);
335:    Ok(path)
336:}
337:
338:/// `ReplaceFileW` can fail after it has already moved the original aside
339:/// (ERROR_UNABLE_TO_MOVE_REPLACEMENT). If the target is gone, the staged file
340:/// is the only copy of the text left and it goes back under the target name;
341:/// only when the original is still there is the staged file litter.
342:fn recover_replacement(target: &Path, temp: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {
343:    if target.exists() {
344:        return match fs::remove_file(temp) {
345:            Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
346:            other => other,
347:        };
348:    }
349:    if temp.exists() {
350:        return fs::rename(temp, target);
351:    }
352:    Ok(())
353:}
354:
355:/// ReplaceFileW keeps the creation time and the attributes of the original;
356:/// a file that is not there yet is just renamed into place (spec §8).
357:fn commit(target: &Path, temp: &Path, existed: bool) -> FsResult<()> {
358:    if !existed {
359:        return fs::rename(temp, target).map_err(FsError::from);
360:    }
361:    let replaced = wide(target);
362:    let replacement = wide(temp);
363:    let outcome = unsafe {
364:        ReplaceFileW(
365:            PCWSTR(replaced.as_ptr()),
366:            PCWSTR(replacement.as_ptr()),
367:            PCWSTR::null(),
368:            REPLACEFILE_IGNORE_MERGE_ERRORS,
369:            None,
370:            None,
371:        )
372:    };
373:    if let Err(error) = outcome {
374:        let message = error.message();
375:        return Err(match recover_replacement(target, temp) {
376:            Ok(()) => FsError::io(message),
377:            Err(problem) => FsError::io(format!(
378:                "{message}; the new text is in {} — {problem}",
379:                temp.display()
380:            )),
381:        });
382:    }
383:    Ok(())
384:}
385:
386:#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
387:#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
388:pub struct WriteRequest {
389:    pub path: String,
390:    pub text: String,
391:    pub encoding: String,
392:    pub bom: bool,
393:    pub eol: String,
394:    /// Hash of the bytes the buffer was loaded from; `null` for Save As.
395:    pub base_hash: Option<String>,
396:    /// The file was deleted from under us and the user wants it back (§8).
397:    #[serde(default)]
398:    pub allow_missing: bool,
399:}
400:
401:#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]
402:pub struct WriteResult {
403:    hash: String,
404:}
405:
406:/// One lock for every write. A personal editor with one window does not need
407:/// per-path locks, and this also keeps two documents in the same folder from
408:/// racing over their temp files.
409:static WRITE_LOCK: Mutex<()> = Mutex::new(());
410:
411:pub fn write_checked(request: &WriteRequest) -> FsResult<String> {
412:    let _guard = WRITE_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
413:    let path = PathBuf::from(&request.path);
414:
415:    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
416:    if request.bom {
417:        bytes.extend_from_slice(bom_bytes(&request.encoding));
418:    }
419:    bytes.extend_from_slice(&encode(
420:        &apply_eol(&request.text, &request.eol),
421:        &request.encoding,
422:    )?);
423:
424:    let dir = path
425:        .parent()
426:        .filter(|parent| !parent.as_os_str().is_empty())
427:        .ok_or_else(|| FsError::io("no parent folder"))?;
428:    // The file is gone and nobody asked for it back: nothing may be created,
429:    // not even the folder it used to live in. Decided before staging, because
430:    // staging is what would put that folder back (spec §8).
431:    let existed = path.exists();
432:    if !existed && request.base_hash.is_some() && !request.allow_missing {
433:        return Err(FsError::missing(&path));
434:    }
435:
436:    // Everything past here is a write the caller is allowed to make, so the
437:    // temp file is prepared now: it makes the gap between checking the file's
438:    // hash and replacing it as short as it can be (spec §8).
439:    let temp = stage(dir, &bytes)?;
440:
441:    let verdict = match (existed, request.base_hash.as_deref()) {
442:        (true, Some(base)) => fs::read(&path).map_err(FsError::from).and_then(|current| {
443:            if blake3::hash(&current).to_hex().to_string() == base {
444:                Ok(())
445:            } else {
446:                Err(FsError::conflict())
447:            }
448:        }),
449:        _ => Ok(()),
450:    };
451:
452:    if let Err(error) = verdict {
453:        let _ = fs::remove_file(&temp);
454:        return Err(error);
455:    }
456:
457:    commit(&path, &temp, existed)?;
458:    Ok(blake3::hash(&bytes).to_hex().to_string())
459:}
460:
461:#[tauri::command]

464:}

496:        WriteRequest {

498:            text: text.into(),

507:    #[test]
508:    fn writes_a_new_file_and_replaces_it() {
509:        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
510:        let file = dir.path().join("note.md");
511:
512:        write_checked(&request(&file, "one\n")).unwrap();
513:        assert_eq!(fs::read(&file).unwrap(), b"one\n");
514:
515:        let hash = write_checked(&request(&file, "two\n")).unwrap();
516:        assert_eq!(fs::read(&file).unwrap(), b"two\n");
517:        assert_eq!(hash, blake3::hash(b"two\n").to_hex().to_string());
518:        // The temp file is gone; the folder holds the note and nothing else.
519:        assert_eq!(fs::read_dir(dir.path()).unwrap().count(), 1);
520:    }

526:        fs::write(&file, b"on disk\n").unwrap();

531:
532:        assert_eq!(error.kind, "conflict");

534:        assert_eq!(fs::read_dir(dir.path()).unwrap().count(), 1);

564:    /// The folder went with the file when it was deleted from outside. A save

566:    /// and no empty folder standing in the tree either (spec §8).
567:    #[test]
568:    fn a_refused_save_creates_nothing_at_all() {
569:        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
570:        let folder = dir.path().join("notes");
571:        let file = folder.join("gone.md");
572:
573:        let mut ask = request(&file, "mine\n");
574:        ask.base_hash = Some(blake3::hash(b"whatever").to_hex().to_string());
575:        assert_eq!(write_checked(&ask).unwrap_err().kind, "missing");
576:        assert!(!folder.exists(), "a refused save put the folder back");
577:        assert_eq!(fs::read_dir(dir.path()).unwrap().count(), 0);
578:
579:        // Saying yes to it is what makes the folder again: `recreated` (§8).
580:        ask.allow_missing = true;
581:        write_checked(&ask).unwrap();
582:        assert!(folder.is_dir());
583:        assert_eq!(fs::read(&file).unwrap(), b"mine\n");
584:    }

616:
617:        let with_bom = dir.path().join("utf8-bom.md");
618:        let mut bytes = vec![0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF];
619:        bytes.extend_from_slice("привет\n".as_bytes());
620:        fs::write(&with_bom, &bytes).unwrap();
621:        let info = read_info(&with_bom, None).unwrap();
622:        assert_eq!(info.encoding, "utf-8");
623:        assert!(info.bom);
624:        assert_eq!(info.text, "привет\n");
625:
626:        let utf16 = dir.path().join("utf16le.md");
627:        let mut bytes = vec![0xFF, 0xFE];
628:        bytes.extend("привет\n".encode_utf16().flat_map(u16::to_le_bytes));
629:        fs::write(&utf16, &bytes).unwrap();
630:        let info = read_info(&utf16, None).unwrap();
631:        assert_eq!(info.encoding, "utf-16le");

633:        assert_eq!(info.text, "привет\n");

636:        let russian = "Это заметка на русском языке, довольно длинная строка.\n";

638:        assert!(!had_errors);

FILE src/ui/MenuBar.tsx
1:// Our own menu bar (spec §4). The native Windows one draws in Segoe UI with
2:// its own capitals; this one is the app's typography. The entries come from
3:// app/menu.ts, which reads the command registry, so nothing is written twice.
4:
5:import { useEffect, useReducer } from "react";
6:import * as Menubar from "@radix-ui/react-menubar";
7:import { menuModel, type MenuNode } from "../app/menu";
8:import { activeDoc, useStore } from "../app/store";
9:import { onLineNumbers } from "../editor/setup";
10:import "./menubar.css";
11:
12:function Row({ node }: { node: MenuNode }) {
13:  if (node.kind === "separator") {
14:    return <Menubar.Separator className="mb-sep" />;
15:  }
16:
17:  if (node.kind === "submenu") {
18:    return (
19:      <Menubar.Sub>
20:        <Menubar.SubTrigger className="mb-item">
21:          <span className="mb-check" />
22:          <span className="mb-label">{node.label}</span>
23:          <span className="mb-arrow">▸</span>
24:        </Menubar.SubTrigger>
25:        <Menubar.Portal>
26:          <Menubar.SubContent className="mb-content" sideOffset={-1} alignOffset={-5}>
27:            {node.items.map((child) => (
28:              <Row key={child.key} node={child} />
29:            ))}
30:          </Menubar.SubContent>
31:        </Menubar.Portal>
32:      </Menubar.Sub>
33:    );
34:  }
35:
36:  return (
37:    <Menubar.Item className="mb-item" disabled={node.disabled} onSelect={() => node.run()}>
38:      <span className="mb-check">{node.checked ? "●" : ""}</span>
39:      <span className="mb-label">{node.label}</span>
40:      {node.chord && <span className="mb-chord">{node.chord}</span>}
41:    </Menubar.Item>
42:  );
43:}
44:
45:export function MenuBar() {
46:  // Only what the model actually reads — the ticks, the recent list and the
47:  // `when()` predicates. Subscribing to the whole store would rebuild the
48:  // model on every keystroke, because the caret lives there too.
49:  useStore((s) => s.recent);
50:  useStore((s) => s.theme);
51:  useStore((s) => s.alwaysOnTop);
52:  useStore((s) => s.activeId);
53:  useStore((s) => s.libraryPath);
54:  useStore((s) => s.treeSelected);
55:  useStore((s) => activeDoc(s)?.mode);
56:  useStore((s) => activeDoc(s)?.path);
57:
58:  // `Ctrl+Shift+9` flips the line numbers outside the store (editor/setup).
59:  const [, bump] = useReducer((n: number) => n + 1, 0);
60:  useEffect(() => onLineNumbers(bump), []);
61:
62:  const sections = menuModel();
63:
64:  return (
65:    <Menubar.Root className="menubar">
66:      {sections.map((section) => (
67:        <Menubar.Menu key={section.key}>
68:          <Menubar.Trigger className="mb-trigger">{section.label}</Menubar.Trigger>
69:          <Menubar.Portal>
70:            <Menubar.Content className="mb-content" align="start" sideOffset={0}>
71:              {section.items.map((node) => (
72:                <Row key={node.key} node={node} />
73:              ))}
74:            </Menubar.Content>
75:          </Menubar.Portal>
76:        </Menubar.Menu>
77:      ))}
78:    </Menubar.Root>
79:  );
80:}

FILE src/app/menu.ts
1:// What the menu bar shows (spec §4), as plain data. The bar itself is our
2:// own markup (ui/MenuBar.tsx) — the native Windows menu draws in Segoe UI
3:// with its own capitals, which does not belong in this app.
4://
5:// Every entry names a command in the registry, so a title and a chord are
6:// written once and the palette, the keyboard layer and the menu agree.
7:
8:import { openPaths } from "./commands";
9:import { basename } from "./paths";
10:import { command } from "./registry";
11:import { reopenAs } from "./save";
12:import type { Theme } from "./settings";
13:import { useStore } from "./store";
14:import { lineNumbersOn } from "../editor/setup";
15:
16:/** Last ten of the twenty the session keeps (spec §4). */
17:const RECENT_IN_MENU = 10;
18:
19:export interface MenuEntry {
20:  kind: "item";
21:  /** Unique inside its list; React keys and nothing else. */
22:  key: string;
23:  label: string;
24:  /** Shown right-aligned, lowercase: `ctrl shift s`. */
25:  chord?: string;
26:  /** Greyed out rather than hidden — the menu is also a map of the app. */
27:  disabled?: boolean;
28:  /** Draws the `●` glyph; theme, line numbers, always on top. */
29:  checked?: boolean;
30:  run: () => void;
31:}
32:
33:export interface MenuSeparator {
34:  kind: "separator";
35:  key: string;
36:}
37:
38:export interface MenuSubmenu {
39:  kind: "submenu";
40:  key: string;
41:  label: string;
42:  items: MenuNode[];
43:}
44:
45:export type MenuNode = MenuEntry | MenuSeparator | MenuSubmenu;
46:
47:export interface MenuSection {
48:  key: string;
49:  label: string;
50:  items: MenuNode[];
51:}
52:
53:/** "Ctrl+Shift+S" -> "ctrl shift s", the way the spec writes chords. */
54:export function chordText(chord: string): string {
55:  return chord
56:    .split("+")
57:    .map((part) => part.trim().toLowerCase())
58:    .join(" ");
59:}
60:
61:let separators = 0;
62:
63:function separator(): MenuSeparator {
64:  separators += 1;
65:  return { kind: "separator", key: `sep-${separators}` };
66:}
67:
68:/** One registry command as an entry; `when()` decides enabled, not visible. */
69:function entry(id: string, label?: string): MenuEntry {
70:  const found = command(id);
71:  const chord = found?.chord ?? found?.hint;
72:  return {
73:    kind: "item",
74:    key: id,
75:    label: label ?? found?.title ?? id,
76:    ...(chord ? { chord: chordText(chord) } : {}),
77:    disabled: found?.when ? !found.when() : false,
78:    run: () => void found?.run(),
79:  };
80:}
81:
82:function recentItems(): MenuNode[] {
83:  const { recent } = useStore.getState();
84:  if (recent.length === 0) {
85:    return [
86:      { kind: "item", key: "recent-empty", label: "nothing yet", disabled: true, run: () => {} },
87:    ];
88:  }
89:  return recent.slice(0, RECENT_IN_MENU).map((path, index) => ({
90:    kind: "item",
91:    key: `recent-${index}-${path}`,
92:    label: basename(path),
93:    run: () => void openPaths([path]),
94:  }));
95:}
96:
97:function reopenItems(): MenuNode[] {
98:  const enabled = command("file.reopenUtf8")?.when?.() ?? true;
99:  const one = (key: string, label: string, encoding: string): MenuEntry => ({
100:    kind: "item",
101:    key,
102:    label,
103:    disabled: !enabled,
104:    run: () => reopenAs(encoding),
105:  });
106:  return [
107:    one("reopen-utf8", "utf-8", "utf-8"),
108:    one("reopen-cp1251", "cp1251", "windows-1251"),
109:    one("reopen-utf16", "utf-16", "utf-16le"),
110:  ];
111:}
112:
113:/** A theme entry carries the `●` for the one in force. */
114:function themeItem(name: Theme, id: string): MenuEntry {
115:  return { ...entry(id, name), key: `theme-${name}`, checked: useStore.getState().theme === name };
116:}
117:
118:/**
119: * The whole bar, rebuilt on every render — it is a few dozen objects, and
120: * this way `disabled` and the ticks are never stale.
121: */
122:export function menuModel(): MenuSection[] {
123:  separators = 0;
124:  const state = useStore.getState();
125:
126:  return [
127:    {
128:      key: "file",
129:      label: "file",
130:      items: [
131:        entry("file.new"),
132:        entry("file.open"),
133:        entry("file.openLibrary"),
134:        { kind: "submenu", key: "recent", label: "recent", items: recentItems() },
135:        separator(),
136:        entry("file.save"),
137:        entry("file.saveAs"),
138:        entry("file.copyPlain"),
139:        {
140:          kind: "submenu",
141:          key: "export",
142:          label: "export",
143:          items: [
144:            entry("file.exportText", "text…"),
145:            entry("file.exportPdf", "pdf…"),
146:          ],
147:        },
148:        { kind: "submenu", key: "reopen", label: "reopen as", items: reopenItems() },
149:        separator(),
150:        entry("file.close"),
151:        entry("file.exit"),
152:      ],
153:    },
154:    {
155:      key: "edit",
156:      // undo/redo run against the live CodeMirror view, not the system edit
157:      // command, which the editor would never see (spec §5.2).
158:      label: "edit",
159:      items: [
160:        entry("edit.undo"),
161:        entry("edit.redo"),
162:        separator(),
163:        entry("nav.find"),
164:        entry("nav.replace"),
165:        separator(),
166:        entry("edit.bold"),
167:        entry("edit.italic"),
168:        entry("edit.code"),
169:        entry("edit.link"),
170:        entry("edit.quote"),
171:        entry("edit.list"),
172:        {
173:          kind: "submenu",
174:          key: "heading",
175:          label: "heading",
176:          items: [
177:            ...[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6].map((level) => entry(`edit.heading${level}`, String(level))),
178:            separator(),
179:            entry("edit.headingOff", "off"),
180:          ],
181:        },
182:      ],
183:    },
184:    {
185:      key: "view",
186:      label: "view",
187:      items: [
188:        entry("view.read"),
189:        entry("view.edit"),
190:        separator(),
191:        entry("view.files"),
192:        entry("view.outline"),
193:        { ...entry("view.lineNumbers"), checked: lineNumbersOn() },
194:        {
195:          kind: "submenu",
196:          key: "theme",
197:          label: "theme",
198:          items: [
199:            themeItem("system", "view.themeSystem"),
200:            themeItem("light", "view.themeLight"),
201:            themeItem("dark", "view.themeDark"),
202:          ],
203:        },
204:        separator(),
205:        { ...entry("view.alwaysOnTop"), checked: state.alwaysOnTop },
206:        entry("view.fullscreen"),
207:        separator(),
208:        entry("view.zoomIn"),
209:        entry("view.zoomOut"),
210:        entry("view.zoomReset"),
211:      ],
212:    },

214:      key: "help",
215:      label: "help",
216:      items: [entry("app.settings"), entry("app.shortcuts"), entry("app.about")],
217:    },
218:  ];

FILE src/ui/Settings.tsx
1:// The settings screen (spec §10, mockup 1e). It sits where the document

14:/** `−`/`+` bounds, from spec §10. */
15:const FONT_SIZE = { min: 11, max: 20, step: 0.5 };
16:const CONTENT_WIDTH = { min: 440, max: 900, step: 20 };
17:
18:function clamp(value: number, min: number, max: number): number {
19:  return Math.min(max, Math.max(min, value));
20:}
21:
22:/** 13.5 stays 13.5, 14.0 becomes 14. */
23:function short(value: number): string {
24:  return String(Math.round(value * 10) / 10);
25:}
26:
27:function Row({
28:  name,
29:  about,
30:  children,
31:}: {
32:  name: string;
33:  about: string;
34:  children: ReactNode;
35:}) {
36:  return (
37:    <div className="setting">
38:      <div className="setting-key">
39:        <span className="setting-name">{name}</span>
40:        <span className="setting-about">{about}</span>
41:      </div>
42:      <div className="setting-control">{children}</div>
43:    </div>
44:  );
45:}
46:
47:/** Words separated by `·`; the one in force is `--fg`, the rest `--muted`. */
48:function Choice<T extends string | number | boolean>({
49:  value,
50:  options,
51:  onPick,
52:}: {
53:  value: T;
54:  options: { label: string; value: T }[];
55:  onPick: (value: T) => void;
56:}) {
57:  return (
58:    <span className="choice">
59:      {options.map((option, index) => (
60:        <Fragment key={option.label}>
61:          {index > 0 && <span className="sep">·</span>}
62:          <button
63:            className={option.value === value ? "choice-option is-on" : "choice-option"}
64:            onClick={() => onPick(option.value)}
65:          >
66:            {option.label}
67:          </button>
68:        </Fragment>
69:      ))}
70:    </span>
71:  );
72:}
73:
74:function Stepper({
75:  value,
76:  bounds,
77:  onChange,
78:}: {
79:  value: number;
80:  bounds: { min: number; max: number; step: number };
81:  onChange: (value: number) => void;
82:}) {
83:  const step = (direction: number) =>
84:    onChange(clamp(value + direction * bounds.step, bounds.min, bounds.max));
85:  return (
86:    <span className="number">
87:      <button className="number-step" onClick={() => step(-1)} title="less">
88:        −
89:      </button>
90:      <span className="number-value">{short(value)}</span>
91:      <button className="number-step" onClick={() => step(1)} title="more">
92:        +
93:      </button>
94:    </span>
95:  );
96:}
97:
98:const ON_OFF = [
99:  { label: "on", value: true },
100:  { label: "off", value: false },
101:];
102:
103:export function SettingsScreen() {
104:  const settings = useStore((s) => s.settings);
105:  const change = useStore((s) => s.changeSettings);
106:  const close = () => useStore.getState().setSettingsOpen(false);
107:
108:  // The extensions field is free text while it is being typed; the settings
109:  // only hear about it once the field is left or Enter is pressed.
110:  const [extensions, setExtensions] = useState(settings.library.extensions.join(", "));
111:  useEffect(() => setExtensions(settings.library.extensions.join(", ")), [settings.library.extensions]);
112:
113:  useEffect(() => {
114:    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
115:      if (event.key !== "Escape") return;
116:      // The `defaults` confirmation is a modal on top; Esc belongs to it.
117:      if (useStore.getState().dialog) return;
118:      event.preventDefault();
119:      event.stopPropagation();
120:      close();
121:    };
122:    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
123:    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
124:  }, []);
125:
126:  const patch = (next: Settings) => change(next);
127:
128:  const commitExtensions = () => {
129:    const list = extensions
130:      .split(",")
131:      .map((item) => item.trim())
132:      .filter((item) => item.length > 0)
133:      .map((item) => (item.startsWith(".") ? item : `.${item}`));
134:    const value = list.length > 0 ? list : DEFAULTS.library.extensions;
135:    setExtensions(value.join(", "));
136:    if (value.join() === settings.library.extensions.join()) return;
137:    patch({ ...settings, library: { extensions: value } });
138:    window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent(TREE_CHANGED));
139:  };
140:
141:  const restoreDefaults = () => {
142:    const store = useStore.getState();
143:    store.setDialog({
144:      title: "reset every setting?",
145:      lines: ["the file goes back to what plain ships with."],
146:      actions: [
147:        {
148:          label: "reset",
149:          run: () => {
150:            store.setDialog(null);
151:            change(DEFAULTS);
152:            syncLineNumbers();
153:            window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent(TREE_CHANGED));
154:          },
155:        },
156:        { label: "cancel", run: () => store.setDialog(null) },
157:      ],
158:      cancel: () => store.setDialog(null),
159:    });
160:  };
161:
162:  return (
163:    <div className="screen">
164:      <div className="screen-column settings-column">
165:        <div className="settings-head">
166:          <h1 className="screen-title">settings</h1>
167:          <button className="link" onClick={close}>
168:            close
169:          </button>
170:        </div>
171:
172:        <Row name="appearance.theme" about="dark follows windows until you pick one">
173:          <Choice
174:            value={settings.appearance.theme}
175:            options={[
176:              { label: "system", value: "system" as const },
177:              { label: "light", value: "light" as const },
178:              { label: "dark", value: "dark" as const },
179:            ]}
180:            onPick={(theme) => useStore.getState().setTheme(theme)}
181:          />
182:        </Row>
183:
184:        <Row name="appearance.fontSize" about="the text of the document, in pixels">
185:          <Stepper
186:            value={settings.appearance.fontSize}
187:            bounds={FONT_SIZE}
188:            onChange={(fontSize) =>
189:              patch({ ...settings, appearance: { ...settings.appearance, fontSize } })
190:            }
191:          />
192:        </Row>
193:
194:        <Row name="appearance.contentWidth" about="width of the read column; edit adds 80">
195:          <Stepper
196:            value={settings.appearance.contentWidth}
197:            bounds={CONTENT_WIDTH}
198:            onChange={(contentWidth) =>
199:              patch({ ...settings, appearance: { ...settings.appearance, contentWidth } })
200:            }
201:          />
202:        </Row>
203:
204:        <Row name="read.codeWrap" about="wrap long lines inside code blocks">
205:          <Choice
206:            value={settings.read.codeWrap}
207:            options={ON_OFF}
208:            onPick={(codeWrap) => patch({ ...settings, read: { codeWrap } })}
209:          />
210:        </Row>
211:
212:        <Row name="edit.lineNumbers" about="ctrl shift 9 still flips them for the session">
213:          <Choice
214:            value={settings.edit.lineNumbers}
215:            options={ON_OFF}
216:            onPick={(lineNumbers) => {
217:              patch({ ...settings, edit: { ...settings.edit, lineNumbers } });
218:              syncLineNumbers();
219:            }}
220:          />
221:        </Row>
222:
223:        <Row name="edit.indentUnit" about="what the file already uses wins over this">
224:          <Choice
225:            value={settings.edit.indentUnit}
226:            options={[
227:              { label: "tab", value: "tab" as const },
228:              { label: "2", value: 2 as const },
229:              { label: "4", value: 4 as const },
230:            ]}
231:            onPick={(indentUnit) => patch({ ...settings, edit: { ...settings.edit, indentUnit } })}
232:          />
233:        </Row>
234:
235:        <Row name="files.newFileEol" about="line endings for files plain creates">
236:          <Choice
237:            value={settings.files.newFileEol}
238:            options={[
239:              { label: "crlf", value: "crlf" as const },
240:              { label: "lf", value: "lf" as const },
241:            ]}
242:            onPick={(newFileEol) => patch({ ...settings, files: { newFileEol } })}
243:          />
244:        </Row>
245:
246:        <Row name="library.extensions" about="what the file tree shows, comma separated">
247:          <input
248:            className="setting-field"
249:            value={extensions}
250:            spellCheck={false}
251:            onChange={(event) => setExtensions(event.target.value)}
252:            onBlur={commitExtensions}
253:            onKeyDown={(event) => {
254:              if (event.key === "Enter") event.currentTarget.blur();
255:            }}
256:          />
257:        </Row>
258:

261:            className="link"

265:            }}

271:            defaults
272:          </button>

276:  );
