FILE src/ui/Shortcuts.tsx
1:// The key list (spec §12), `F1` and `help → shortcuts`. It used to live in
2:// the README; it belongs where the keys are. Everything with a chord comes
3:// out of the command registry, so this page cannot drift from the bindings.
4:
5:import { Fragment, useEffect } from "react";
6:import { chordText } from "../app/menu";
7:import { commands } from "../app/registry";
8:import { useStore } from "../app/store";
9:import "./dialogs.css";
10:import "./settings.css";
11:
12:type Group =
13:  | "files"
14:  | "navigation"
15:  | "view"
16:  | "formatting"
17:  | "editing"
18:  | "tree"
19:  | "other";
20:
21:const ORDER: Group[] = [
22:  "files",
23:  "navigation",
24:  "view",
25:  "formatting",
26:  "editing",
27:  "tree",
28:  "other",
29:];
30:
31:/** Command id prefix -> the group it is listed under. */
32:const BY_PREFIX: { prefix: string; group: Group }[] = [
33:  { prefix: "file.", group: "files" },
34:  { prefix: "nav.", group: "navigation" },
35:  { prefix: "view.", group: "view" },
36:  { prefix: "edit.", group: "formatting" },
37:  { prefix: "app.", group: "other" },
38:];
39:
40:/** Undo and redo are `edit.` ids but they are not formatting. */
41:const MOVED: Record<string, Group> = { "edit.undo": "editing", "edit.redo": "editing" };
42:
43:/** `Ctrl+1…6` is one line in the spec; six rows would only be noise. */
44:const HEADINGS = "edit.heading1";
45:const SKIP = new Set(["edit.heading2", "edit.heading3", "edit.heading4", "edit.heading5", "edit.heading6"]);
46:
47:interface Row {
48:  key: string;
49:  label: string;
50:  keys: string;
51:}
52:
53:/**
54: * Bindings that belong to CodeMirror's own keymap (@codemirror/commands and
55: * @codemirror/search), not to our registry — they are standard CM6 and are
56: * listed here by hand so the page is the whole truth (spec §5.2).
57: */
58:const STANDARD: Record<string, Row[]> = {
59:  editing: [
60:    { key: "cm-multi", label: "select next occurrence", keys: "ctrl d" },
61:    { key: "cm-cursor", label: "add cursor above / below", keys: "ctrl alt ↑ ↓" },
62:    { key: "cm-move", label: "move line", keys: "alt ↑ ↓" },
63:    { key: "cm-dup", label: "duplicate line", keys: "alt shift ↑ ↓" },
64:    { key: "cm-del", label: "delete line", keys: "ctrl shift x" },
65:    { key: "cm-fold", label: "fold / unfold", keys: "ctrl shift [ ]" },
66:    { key: "cm-plain", label: "paste as plain text", keys: "ctrl shift v" },
67:    { key: "cm-tab", label: "indent / outdent list item", keys: "tab · shift tab" },
68:  ],
69:  tree: [
70:    { key: "tree-open", label: "open", keys: "enter" },
71:    { key: "tree-move", label: "move", keys: "↑ ↓" },
72:    { key: "tree-rename", label: "rename", keys: "f2" },
73:    { key: "tree-delete", label: "delete to recycle bin", keys: "delete" },
74:  ],
75:};
76:
77:function groupOf(id: string): Group {
78:  const moved = MOVED[id];
79:  if (moved) return moved;
80:  return BY_PREFIX.find((entry) => id.startsWith(entry.prefix))?.group ?? "other";
81:}
82:
83:function rows(): Record<Group, Row[]> {
84:  const out = Object.fromEntries(ORDER.map((g) => [g, [] as Row[]])) as Record<Group, Row[]>;
85:
86:  for (const command of commands) {
87:    // `hint` is a chord the editor's keymap or WebView2 owns; it is still a
88:    // key the reader can press, so it belongs on this page.
89:    const chord = command.chord ?? command.hint;
90:    if (!chord || SKIP.has(command.id)) continue;
91:    const heading = command.id === HEADINGS;
92:    out[groupOf(command.id)].push({
93:      key: command.id,
94:      label: heading ? "heading 1–6" : command.title,
95:      keys: heading ? "ctrl 1 … ctrl 6" : chordText(chord),
96:    });
97:  }
98:
99:  for (const [group, list] of Object.entries(STANDARD)) {
100:    out[group as Group].push(...list);
101:  }
102:  return out;
103:}
104:
105:export function ShortcutsScreen() {
106:  const close = () => useStore.getState().setShortcutsOpen(false);
107:
108:  useEffect(() => {
109:    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
110:      if (event.key !== "Escape") return;
111:      if (useStore.getState().dialog) return;
112:      event.preventDefault();
113:      event.stopPropagation();
114:      close();
115:    };
116:    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
117:    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
118:  }, []);
119:
120:  const grouped = rows();
121:
122:  return (
123:    <div className="screen">
124:      <div className="screen-column settings-column">
125:        <div className="settings-head">
126:          <h1 className="screen-title">shortcuts</h1>
127:          <button className="link" onClick={close}>
128:            close
129:          </button>
130:        </div>
131:
132:        {ORDER.map((group) => {
133:          const list = grouped[group];
134:          if (list.length === 0) return null;
135:          return (
136:            <Fragment key={group}>
137:              <div className="keys-group section-title">{group}</div>
138:              {list.map((row) => (
139:                <div className="keys-row" key={row.key}>
140:                  <span className="keys-what">{row.label}</span>
141:                  <span className="keys-chord">{row.keys}</span>
142:                </div>
143:              ))}
144:            </Fragment>
145:          );
146:        })}
147:
148:        <div className="keys-note">shortcuts work in any keyboard layout</div>
149:      </div>
150:    </div>
151:  );
152:}

FILE src/editor/toolbar.ts
1:// The floating format panel (spec §5.2, mockup 1d): a dark 30px strip above
2:// a selection made with the mouse. Keyboard selections do not get it — for
3:// those there are chords. One instance, owned by the view that made it.
4:
5:import type { Extension } from "@codemirror/state";
6:import { EditorView, ViewPlugin, type ViewUpdate } from "@codemirror/view";
7:import {
8:  insertLink,
9:  toggleBold,
10:  toggleInlineCode,
11:  toggleItalic,
12:  toggleQuote,
13:  toggleStrike,
14:} from "./commands";
15:
16:/** Mockup 1d: the strip sits 38px above the top of the selection. */
17:const OFFSET = 38;
18:/** No room above (the top visible line): it goes under the selection instead. */
19:const BELOW = 8;
20:
21:interface Button {
22:  label: string;
23:  title: string;
24:  className: string;
25:  run: (view: EditorView) => boolean;
26:}
27:
28:const BUTTONS: Button[] = [
29:  { label: "B", title: "bold", className: "tb-bold", run: toggleBold },
30:  { label: "I", title: "italic", className: "tb-italic", run: toggleItalic },
31:  { label: "S", title: "strikethrough", className: "tb-strike", run: toggleStrike },
32:  { label: "`", title: "code", className: "tb-code", run: toggleInlineCode },
33:  { label: "link", title: "link", className: "", run: insertLink },
34:  { label: "quote", title: "quote", className: "", run: toggleQuote },
35:];
36:
37:/** `link` and `quote` sit behind the separator, as in the mockup. */
38:const SEPARATOR_BEFORE = 4;
39:
40:class FormatToolbar {
41:  private dom: HTMLElement | null = null;
42:  /** Only the newest pending mouseup may place the panel. */
43:  private token = 0;
44:  /** A click on a button ends in a mouseup that must not bring it back. */
45:  private suppress = false;
46:
47:  constructor(private view: EditorView) {
48:    view.dom.addEventListener("mouseup", this.onMouseUp);
49:    view.scrollDOM.addEventListener("scroll", this.hide);
50:    view.dom.addEventListener("focusout", this.onFocusOut);
51:    window.addEventListener("resize", this.hide);
52:  }
53:
54:  update(update: ViewUpdate): void {
55:    // Typing, moving the caret and dragging a selection all take it away;
56:    // the mouseup that ends a drag is what brings it back.
57:    if (update.docChanged || update.selectionSet || update.focusChanged) this.hide();
58:  }
59:
60:  destroy(): void {
61:    this.view.dom.removeEventListener("mouseup", this.onMouseUp);
62:    this.view.scrollDOM.removeEventListener("scroll", this.hide);
63:    this.view.dom.removeEventListener("focusout", this.onFocusOut);
64:    window.removeEventListener("resize", this.hide);
65:    this.hide();
66:  }
67:
68:  private onFocusOut = (event: FocusEvent): void => {
69:    // Clicking a button moves the focus inside the panel; that is not a blur.
70:    const next = event.relatedTarget;
71:    if (next instanceof Node && this.dom?.contains(next)) return;
72:    this.hide();
73:  };
74:
75:  // CodeMirror settles the selection in its own mouseup handler, so the panel
76:  // is placed one task later, when the selection is the final one.
77:  private onMouseUp = (): void => {
78:    if (this.suppress) {
79:      this.suppress = false;
80:      return;
81:    }
82:    const token = ++this.token;
83:    window.setTimeout(() => {
84:      if (token === this.token) this.show();
85:    }, 0);
86:  };
87:
88:  private hide = (): void => {
89:    this.token += 1;
90:    this.dom?.remove();
91:    this.dom = null;
92:  };
93:
94:  private build(): HTMLElement {
95:    const dom = document.createElement("div");
96:    dom.className = "format-toolbar";
97:    BUTTONS.forEach((button, index) => {
98:      if (index === SEPARATOR_BEFORE) {
99:        const separator = document.createElement("span");
100:        separator.className = "tb-sep";
101:        dom.append(separator);
102:      }
103:      const element = document.createElement("button");
104:      if (button.className) element.className = button.className;
105:      element.title = button.title;
106:      element.textContent = button.label;
107:      // mousedown, not click: the default would take the selection away
108:      // before the command has anything to work on.
109:      element.addEventListener("mousedown", (event) => {
110:        event.preventDefault();
111:        this.suppress = true;
112:        this.hide();
113:        button.run(this.view);
114:        this.view.focus();
115:      });
116:      dom.append(element);
117:    });
118:    return dom;
119:  }
120:
121:  private show(): void {
122:    const { main } = this.view.state.selection;
123:    if (main.empty || !this.view.hasFocus) return;
124:
125:    const coords = this.view.coordsAtPos(main.from);
126:    if (!coords) return;
127:
128:    const dom = this.dom ?? this.build();
129:    if (!dom.isConnected) this.view.dom.append(dom);
130:    this.dom = dom;
131:
132:    // coordsAtPos is in client space; the panel is absolute inside .cm-editor.
133:    const host = this.view.dom.getBoundingClientRect();
134:    const left = Math.min(
135:      Math.max(0, coords.left - host.left),
136:      Math.max(0, host.width - dom.offsetWidth),
137:    );
138:    // On the top visible line there is nothing above but the mode bar, so the
139:    // panel goes under the selection instead of over it.
140:    const above = coords.top - host.top - OFFSET;
141:    const top = above >= 0 ? above : coords.bottom - host.top + BELOW;
142:    dom.style.left = `${Math.round(left)}px`;
143:    dom.style.top = `${Math.round(top)}px`;
144:  }
145:}
146:
147:export const formatToolbar: Extension = ViewPlugin.fromClass(FormatToolbar);

FILE src/editor/setup.ts
1:// One place that assembles the CodeMirror extensions. The only compartment

47:export const gutterConf = new Compartment();
48:
49:/* ------------------------------------------------- soft wrap continuation */
50:
51:// Leading blockquote/list markup, so a wrapped line lines up with its text.
52:const MARKUP = /^(?:[ \t]*(?:>[ \t]?|[-*+][ \t]+|\d+[.)][ \t]+))*[ \t]*/;
53:
54:/** Last line of a leading `---` block; 0 when the file has no front matter. */
55:function frontMatterEnd(state: EditorState): number {
56:  if (state.doc.lines < 2 || !/^---\s*$/.test(state.doc.line(1).text)) return 0;
57:  const limit = Math.min(state.doc.lines, 200);
58:  for (let n = 2; n <= limit; n += 1) {
59:    if (/^(---|\.\.\.)\s*$/.test(state.doc.line(n).text)) return n;
60:  }
61:  return 0;
62:}
63:
64:/** Fenced code, so the whole block gets one background like read does. */
65:function inCode(state: EditorState, pos: number): boolean {
66:  for (let node: SyntaxNode | null = syntaxTree(state).resolveInner(pos, 1); node; node = node.parent) {
67:    if (node.name === "FencedCode" || node.name === "CodeBlock") return true;
68:  }
69:  return false;
70:}
71:
72:function lineDecorations(view: EditorView): DecorationSet {
73:  const builder = new RangeSetBuilder<Decoration>();
74:  const { state } = view;
75:  const frontMatter = frontMatterEnd(state);
76:  for (const { from, to } of view.visibleRanges) {
77:    for (let pos = from; pos <= to; ) {
78:      const line = state.doc.lineAt(pos);
79:      const markup = MARKUP.exec(line.text)?.[0] ?? "";
80:      const width = markup ? Math.min(countColumn(markup, state.tabSize), 24) : 0;
81:      const cls =
82:        line.number <= frontMatter
83:          ? "cm-frontmatter"
84:          : inCode(state, line.from)
85:            ? "cm-code"
86:            : "";
87:      if (width > 0 || cls) {
88:        builder.add(
89:          line.from,
90:          line.from,
91:          Decoration.line({
92:            class: cls || undefined,
93:            attributes:
94:              width > 0
95:                ? { style: `text-indent:-${width}ch;padding-left:${width}ch` }
96:                : undefined,
97:          }),
98:        );
99:      }
100:      pos = line.to + 1;
101:    }
102:  }
103:  return builder.finish();
104:}
105:
106:const lineLook = ViewPlugin.fromClass(
107:  class {
108:    decorations: DecorationSet;
109:    constructor(view: EditorView) {
110:      this.decorations = lineDecorations(view);
111:    }
112:    update(update: ViewUpdate) {
113:      if (update.docChanged || update.viewportChanged) {
114:        this.decorations = lineDecorations(update.view);
115:      }
116:    }
117:  },
118:  { decorations: (v) => v.decorations },
119:);
120:
121:/* ----------------------------------------------------- line numbers (§12) */
122:
123:// Ctrl+Shift+9 flips them for the session; settings.json is the starting point.
124:let numbersOverride: boolean | null = null;
125:const numbersListeners = new Set<() => void>();
126:
127:export function lineNumbersOn(): boolean {
128:  return numbersOverride ?? useStore.getState().settings.edit.lineNumbers;
129:}
130:
131:export function toggleLineNumbers(): void {
132:  numbersOverride = !lineNumbersOn();
133:  for (const listener of numbersListeners) listener();
134:}
135:
136:export function onLineNumbers(listener: () => void): () => void {
137:  numbersListeners.add(listener);
138:  return () => numbersListeners.delete(listener);
139:}
140:
141:/**
142: * The settings screen moved the base, so the session override is stale — the
143: * setting is what the user just looked at (spec §10).
144: */
145:export function syncLineNumbers(): void {
146:  numbersOverride = null;
147:  for (const listener of numbersListeners) listener();
148:}
149:
150:export function gutterExtension(on: boolean): Extension {
151:  return on ? lineNumbers() : [];
152:}

157:  typeof window !== "undefined" &&
158:  window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").matches;

172:const REGISTRY_CHORDS = new Set(["Mod-f", "Mod-h", "F3", "Shift-F3", "Mod-g", "Shift-Mod-g", "Mod-Alt-g"]);
173:const ownSearchKeymap = searchKeymap.filter((binding) => !REGISTRY_CHORDS.has(binding.key ?? ""));

175:/**
176: * A URL pasted over selected text becomes a link (spec §5.2). This replaces
177: * the language pack's own handler so it also works in a file too big for
178: * highlighting.
179: */
180:const pasteLink = EditorView.domEventHandlers({
181:  paste(event, view) {
182:    const pasted = event.clipboardData?.getData("text/plain");
183:    if (!pasted) return false;
184:    const spec = urlPaste(view.state, pasted);
185:    if (!spec) return false;
186:    event.preventDefault();
187:    view.dispatch(spec);
188:    return true;
189:  },
190:});

198:      codeLanguages: languages,

207:  const unit = settings.edit.indentUnit === "tab" ? "\t" : " ".repeat(settings.edit.indentUnit);

216:    EditorState.readOnly.of(doc.readOnly),

218:    EditorState.phrases.of({ "Go to line": "go to line", go: "go", close: "×" }),
219:    EditorView.lineWrapping,

FILE src/editor/buffers.ts
1:// One EditorState per document, kept outside React so that leaving edit and
2:// coming back keeps history, selection and scroll (spec §5.0).
3:
4:import { EditorState } from "@codemirror/state";
5:import { normalizeEol } from "../app/eol";
6:import type { Doc } from "../app/store";
7:import { useStore } from "../app/store";
8:import type { Settings } from "../app/settings";
9:import { editorExtensions } from "./setup";
10:
11:export interface Buffer {
12:  state: EditorState;
13:  /**
14:   * The text on disk as CodeMirror holds it — line endings normalized to
15:   * `\n`. `dirty` is a comparison against this, so both sides must be
16:   * normalized or a CRLF file would never look clean again. Saving writes
17:   * the file back in its own dominant style (§8).
18:   */
19:  savedText: string;
20:  scrollTop: number;
21:}
22:
23:const buffers = new Map<string, Buffer>();
24:
25:/** The buffer for a document, created from `doc.text` on first use. */
26:export function buffer(doc: Doc, settings: Settings): Buffer {
27:  const existing = buffers.get(doc.id);
28:  if (existing) return existing;
29:  const state = EditorState.create({
30:    doc: doc.text,
31:    extensions: editorExtensions(doc, settings),
32:  });
33:  // Not the text we just loaded: a restored draft is already unsaved work,
34:  // and its clean text is whatever the file on disk holds (spec §8).
35:  const created: Buffer = {
36:    state,
37:    savedText: normalizeEol(doc.savedText),
38:    scrollTop: 0,
39:  };
40:  buffers.set(doc.id, created);
41:  return created;
42:}
43:
44:export function keepBuffer(id: string, state: EditorState, scrollTop: number): void {
45:  const existing = buffers.get(id);
46:  if (existing) {
47:    existing.state = state;
48:    existing.scrollTop = scrollTop;
49:  }
50:}
51:
52:/** Called after a successful save, with the text that reached the disk. */
53:export function markSaved(id: string, text: string): void {
54:  const existing = buffers.get(id);
55:  if (existing) existing.savedText = normalizeEol(text);
56:}
57:
58:/**
59: * The one dirty test. A document that has never been in edit has no buffer,
60: * so the document's own copy of the disk text answers for it.
61: */
62:export function isDirty(id: string, text: string): boolean {
63:  const existing = buffers.get(id);
64:  if (existing) return existing.savedText !== normalizeEol(text);
65:  const doc = useStore.getState().docs.find((d) => d.id === id);
66:  return doc ? normalizeEol(doc.savedText) !== normalizeEol(text) : false;
67:}
68:
69:/** An external reload, applied as one change so undo has a single step. */
70:export function setBufferText(id: string, text: string): void {
71:  const existing = buffers.get(id);
72:  if (!existing) return;
73:  existing.state = existing.state.update({
74:    changes: { from: 0, to: existing.state.doc.length, insert: text },
75:  }).state;
76:}
77:
78:/** Save As keeps the buffer — history and all — under the new file's id. */
79:export function moveBuffer(from: string, to: string): void {
80:  const existing = buffers.get(from);
81:  if (!existing || from === to) return;
82:  buffers.delete(from);
83:  buffers.set(to, existing);
84:}
85:
86:export function dropBuffer(id: string): void {
87:  buffers.delete(id);
88:}
89:
90:// A closed document must not leave its buffer behind: opening the file again
91:// has to read it from disk, not from a state we kept.
92:useStore.subscribe((state, previous) => {
93:  if (state.docs.length >= previous.docs.length) return;
94:  for (const id of buffers.keys()) {
95:    if (!state.docs.some((d) => d.id === id)) buffers.delete(id);
96:  }
97:});
98:
99:/** Tests only. */
100:export function clearBuffers(): void {
101:  buffers.clear();
102:}

FILE src/read/pipeline.ts
1:// The one markdown pipeline (spec §5.1, §11, §14).
2://
3://   remark-parse + gfm + math + front matter + wikilinks + callouts + ==mark==
4://   -> remark-rehype (dangerous html) -> rehype-raw -> rehype-sanitize
5://   -> rehype-slug -> KaTeX -> code frames -> HTML string
6://
7:// Everything here is synchronous: read is built on entering read, once.
8:// Shiki and Mermaid are async and live in the DOM pass (highlight.ts,
9:// mermaid.ts), because they cannot run inside `processSync`.

11:import katex from "katex";
12:import rehypeRaw from "rehype-raw";

27:export interface RenderResult {
28:  html: string;
29:  headings: Heading[];
30:  words: number;
31:  frontmatter: Record<string, unknown> | null;
32:}

34:/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ utils */
35:
36:function classList(node: Element): string[] {
37:  const value: unknown = node.properties?.["className"];
38:  if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(String);
39:  return typeof value === "string" ? value.split(/\s+/) : [];
40:}
41:
42:/** Plain text of a hast subtree; `raw` nodes (KaTeX output) count as text. */
43:function textOf(node: unknown): string {
44:  const it = node as { type?: string; value?: unknown; children?: unknown };
45:  if (it.type === "comment" || it.type === "doctype") return "";
46:  if (typeof it.value === "string") return it.value;
47:  return Array.isArray(it.children) ? it.children.map(textOf).join("") : "";
48:}
49:
50:function element(
51:  tagName: string,
52:  properties: Properties,
53:  children: ElementContent[],
54:): Element {
55:  return { type: "element", tagName, properties, children };
56:}
57:
58:/* ----------------------------------------------------------------- schema */
59:
60:const base = defaultSchema;
61:const baseAttrs = base.attributes ?? {};
62:
63:/**
64: * GitHub schema plus exactly what our own constructs need. `clobber` is off:
65: * ids come from our own markdown (rehype-slug, GFM footnotes), and prefixing
66: * them would break every footnote link.
67: */
68:const schema: SanitizeSchema = {
69:  ...base,
70:  clobber: [],
71:  strip: ["script", "style", "iframe", "object", "embed", "form", "textarea", "title"],
72:  // `picture`/`source` are dropped on purpose: `srcset` is a second way to
73:  // fetch an image, and it would walk straight past the deferred `data-src`
74:  // that keeps read off the network (review #2, spec §1.3).
75:  tagNames: [...(base.tagNames ?? []).filter((tag) => tag !== "picture" && tag !== "source"), "mark"],
76:  attributes: {
77:    ...baseAttrs,
78:    // One entry per property name: hast-util-sanitize stops at the first
79:    // definition it finds, so the GitHub `className` rule has to be merged
80:    // rather than appended to.
81:    a: [
82:      "ariaDescribedBy",
83:      "ariaLabel",
84:      "ariaLabelledBy",
85:      "dataFootnoteBackref",
86:      "dataFootnoteRef",
87:      ["className", "data-footnote-backref", "wikilink"],
88:      "href",
89:      "dataWiki",
90:      "dataWikiHash",
91:    ],
92:    code: [["className", /^language-./, "math-inline", "math-display"], "dataLang"],
93:    div: [
94:      ...(baseAttrs["div"] ?? []),
95:      ["className", "callout", "callout-title", "footnotes-label"],
96:      "dataCallout",
97:    ],
98:    span: [["className", "callout-type", "callout-heading"]],
99:    // `src` never survives the pipeline: rehypeDeferImages moves it out of
100:    // the way so nothing is fetched before the reader asks for it (spec §1.3).
101:    img: [
102:      ...(baseAttrs["img"] ?? []).filter((attr) => attr !== "srcSet" && attr !== "sizes"),
103:      "dataSrc",
104:    ],
105:  },
106:};
107:
108:/* ---------------------------------------------------------------- plugins */
109:
110:function frontmatterOf(tree: MdastRoot): Record<string, unknown> | null {
111:  const node = tree.children.find((child) => child.type === "yaml");
112:  if (!node || node.type !== "yaml") return null;
113:  try {
114:    const parsed: unknown = parseYaml(node.value);
115:    return parsed && typeof parsed === "object" && !Array.isArray(parsed)
116:      ? (parsed as Record<string, unknown>)
117:      : null;
118:  } catch {
119:    return null;
120:  }
121:}
122:
123:/** Parks every image source in `data-src`; the DOM pass decides what loads. */
124:const rehypeDeferImages: Plugin<[], HastRoot> = () => (tree) => {
125:  visit(tree, "element", (node) => {
126:    if (node.tagName !== "img" || !node.properties) return;
127:    const src = node.properties["src"];
128:    if (typeof src === "string") node.properties["dataSrc"] = src;
129:    delete node.properties["src"];
130:  });
131:};
132:
133:function renderMath(value: string, display: boolean): ElementContent {
134:  const html = katex.renderToString(value.trim(), {
135:    displayMode: display,
136:    throwOnError: false,
137:    output: "html",
138:    strict: false,
139:  });
140:  return { type: "raw", value: html } as unknown as ElementContent;
141:}
142:
143:/**
144: * Runs after sanitize on purpose: KaTeX leans on inline `style` for its
145: * spacing, and the GitHub schema strips that attribute.
146: */
147:const rehypeKatex: Plugin<[], HastRoot> = () => (tree) => {
148:  visit(tree, "element", (node, index, parent) => {
149:    if (!parent || index === undefined) return;
150:
151:    if (node.tagName === "pre") {
152:      const child = node.children.find((c) => c.type === "element");
153:      if (child?.type !== "element" || !classList(child).includes("math-display")) return;
154:      parent.children[index] = renderMath(textOf(child), true);
155:      return SKIP;
156:    }
157:
158:    if (node.tagName === "code" && classList(node).includes("math-inline")) {
159:      parent.children[index] = renderMath(textOf(node), false);
160:      return SKIP;
161:    }
162:
163:    return;
164:  });
165:};
166:
167:/** Wraps every code block in a frame with a language label and `copy`. */
168:const rehypeCodeFrame: Plugin<[], HastRoot> = () => (tree) => {
169:  visit(tree, "element", (node, index, parent) => {
170:    if (!parent || index === undefined || node.tagName !== "pre") return;
171:
172:    const code = node.children.find((c) => c.type === "element" && c.tagName === "code");
173:    if (code?.type !== "element") return;
174:
175:    const lang =
176:      classList(code)
177:        .find((name) => name.startsWith("language-"))
178:        ?.slice("language-".length) ?? "";
179:    const mermaid = lang === "mermaid";
180:
181:    if (code.properties) code.properties["dataLang"] = lang;
182:
183:    const bar = element("div", { className: ["code-bar"] }, [
184:      element("span", { className: ["code-lang"] }, [{ type: "text", value: lang || "text" }]),
185:      element("button", { className: ["code-copy"], type: "button" }, [
186:        { type: "text", value: "copy" },
187:      ]),
188:    ]);
189:
190:    parent.children[index] = element(
191:      "div",
192:      {
193:        className: mermaid ? ["codeblock", "mermaid-block"] : ["codeblock"],
194:        dataLang: lang,
195:      },
196:      [bar, node],
197:    );
198:    return SKIP;
199:  });
200:};
201:
202:/* ----------------------------------------------------------------- render */
203:
204:/**
205: * Headings that markdown produced already carry an id (`stampHeadings`); this
206: * covers the ones written as raw HTML, using the *same* slugger, so a
207: * `<h2>Duplicate</h2>` after a `## Duplicate` gets `duplicate-1` rather than
208: * a second `duplicate`.
209: */
210:const rehypeHeadingIds: Plugin<[{ slugger: GithubSlugger | null }], HastRoot> =
211:  (box) => (tree) => {
212:    visit(tree, "element", (node) => {
213:      if (!/^h[1-6]$/.test(node.tagName)) return;
214:      const properties = (node.properties ??= {});
215:      if (typeof properties["id"] === "string" && properties["id"] !== "") return;
216:      properties["id"] = box.slugger?.slug(textOf(node)) ?? "";
217:    });
218:  };
219:
220:// `render` is synchronous and single-threaded, so one box is enough to hand
221:// the document's slugger to a processor that is built once.
222:const slugs: { slugger: GithubSlugger | null } = { slugger: null };
223:
224:/** mdast -> HTML. Built once; `render` runs it over an already parsed tree. */
225:const html = unified()
226:  .use(remarkRehype, {
227:    allowDangerousHtml: true,
228:    footnoteLabel: "footnotes",
229:    footnoteLabelTagName: "div",
230:    footnoteLabelProperties: { className: ["footnotes-label"] },
231:  })
232:  .use(rehypeRaw)
233:  .use(rehypeDeferImages)
234:  .use(rehypeSanitize, schema)
235:  .use(rehypeHeadingIds, slugs)
236:  .use(rehypeKatex)
237:  .use(rehypeCodeFrame)
238:  .use(rehypeStringify, { allowDangerousHtml: true })
239:  .freeze();
240:
241:// The most expensive thing this app does, and React will ask for it twice:
242:// strict mode invokes a memo a second time, and a memo is a hint, not a
243:// promise. The result is immutable to callers, so one entry can be shared.
244:let lastText: string | null = null;
245:let lastResult: RenderResult | null = null;
246:
247:export function render(text: string): RenderResult {
248:  if (lastText === text && lastResult) return lastResult;
249:  const result = renderInner(text);
250:  lastText = text;
251:  lastResult = result;
252:  return result;
253:}
254:
255:function renderInner(text: string): RenderResult {
256:  // The Markdown half is `parseDocument`, shared with the outline and the
257:  // word count: one micromark pass per document, which is by far the most
258:  // expensive thing here. This side only turns that tree into HTML.
259:  const tree = parseDocument(text);
260:  const slugger = new GithubSlugger();
261:  const headings = stampHeadings(tree, slugger);
262:  slugs.slugger = slugger;
263:  const hast = html.runSync(tree as never, text);
264:  slugs.slugger = null;
265:
266:  return {
267:    html: html.stringify(hast),
268:    headings,
269:    words: countWordsOf(tree),
270:    frontmatter: frontmatterOf(tree),
271:  };
272:}

FILE src/read/markdown.ts
1:// The Markdown half of read, shared by everything that has to agree about
2:// what a document says: the full pipeline, `headingsOf` and the word count.
3://
4:// One list, one meaning. If edit and read parsed with different plugin sets
5:// they would disagree about heading text and ids (review #9, #15).
6:
7:import remarkFrontmatter from "remark-frontmatter";
8:import remarkGfm from "remark-gfm";
9:import remarkMarkers from "remark-flexible-markers";
10:import remarkMath from "remark-math";
11:import remarkParse from "remark-parse";
12:import { visit, SKIP } from "unist-util-visit";
13:import type { Plugin, Preset } from "unified";
14:import type { Root } from "mdast";
15:import { remarkCallouts } from "./callout";
16:import { remarkWikiLink } from "./wikilink";
17:
18:/**
19: * Currency, not maths (spec §11): a single-dollar span only counts when there
20: * is no space just inside either delimiter and no digit right after the
21: * closing one. `$5 и $10` fails both tests and goes back to being text.
22: */
23:export const remarkMathGuard: Plugin<[], Root> = () => (tree, file) => {
24:  const source = String(file);
25:
26:  visit(tree, "inlineMath", (node, index, parent) => {
27:    if (!parent || index === undefined) return;
28:    const start = node.position?.start.offset;
29:    const end = node.position?.end.offset;
30:    if (start === undefined || end === undefined) return;
31:
32:    const raw = source.slice(start, end);
33:    let fence = 0;
34:    while (raw[fence] === "$") fence += 1;
35:    if (fence !== 1) return; // `$$…$$` inline is never ambiguous
36:
37:    const inner = raw.slice(1, -1);
38:    const after = source.slice(end, end + 1);
39:    const ok = inner !== "" && !/^\s/.test(inner) && !/\s$/.test(inner) && !/[0-9]/.test(after);
40:    if (ok) return;
41:
42:    parent.children[index] = { type: "text", value: raw };
43:    return SKIP;
44:  });
45:};
46:
47:/** Everything that runs before the tree becomes HTML. */
48:export const markdownPreset: Preset = {
49:  plugins: [
50:    remarkParse,
51:    [remarkFrontmatter, ["yaml"]],
52:    remarkGfm,
53:    [remarkMath, { singleDollarTextMath: true }],
54:    remarkMathGuard,
55:    [remarkMarkers, { markerTagName: "mark", markerClassName: () => [] }],
56:    remarkWikiLink,
57:    remarkCallouts,
58:  ],
59:};

FILE src/read/headings.ts
1:// Headings, slugs and source lines from one Markdown parse.
2://
3:// Read and edit must agree on every id, or "jump to the same heading" lands
4:// somewhere else (review #9). So there is one text extractor and one slugger,
5:// used both by the read pipeline (which stamps the ids onto the HTML) and by
6:// `headingsOf`, the cheap parse-only pass the editor calls: remark-parse plus
7:// the extensions that can change what a heading *says* — no rehype, no
8:// KaTeX, no HTML.
9:
10:import GithubSlugger from "github-slugger";
11:import { unified } from "unified";
12:import { visit } from "unist-util-visit";
13:import type { Root } from "mdast";
14:import type { Heading } from "../app/store";
15:import { markdownPreset } from "./markdown";
16:import { displayText, type WikiLink } from "./wikilink";
17:
18:/**
19: * Text of an mdast subtree the way read renders it: a wikilink reads as its
20: * label, raw HTML tags and images contribute nothing, and a formula counts as
21: * its source (the `$` are punctuation the slugger drops either way).
22: */
23:export function nodeText(node: unknown): string {
24:  const it = node as {
25:    type?: string;
26:    value?: unknown;
27:    children?: unknown;
28:  };
29:
30:  if (it.type === "wikiLink") return displayText(node as WikiLink);
31:  if (it.type === "html" || it.type === "image" || it.type === "imageReference") return "";
32:  if (Array.isArray(it.children)) return it.children.map(nodeText).join("");
33:  return typeof it.value === "string" ? it.value : "";
34:}
35:
36:/**
37: * Walks headings in document order. The slugger comes from outside, because
38: * a document has exactly one: headings written as raw HTML are slugged later,
39: * against the same instance, or two headings end up sharing an id.
40: */
41:function collect(tree: Root, slugger: GithubSlugger, stampIds: boolean): Heading[] {
42:  const out: Heading[] = [];
43:
44:  visit(tree, "heading", (node) => {
45:    const text = nodeText(node);
46:    const id = slugger.slug(text);
47:    if (stampIds) {
48:      const data = (node.data ??= {});
49:      data.hProperties = { ...data.hProperties, id };
50:    }
51:    out.push({
52:      level: node.depth,
53:      text: text.trim(),
54:      id,
55:      line: node.position?.start.line ?? 1,
56:    });
57:  });
58:
59:  return out;
60:}
61:
62:/**
63: * The read pipeline's half: ids go on the nodes here rather than in
64: * rehype-slug, so the HTML carries exactly what `headingsOf` reports.
65: * Stamping is idempotent — the same text always produces the same ids — so
66: * it is safe on the tree `parseDocument` hands round.
67: */
68:export function stampHeadings(tree: Root, slugger: GithubSlugger): Heading[] {
69:  return collect(tree, slugger, true);
70:}
71:
72:// Built once, from the same plugin list the full pipeline uses.
73:const processor = unified().use(markdownPreset).freeze();
74:
75:// The outline and the word count ask for the same document one after the
76:// other, and a re-render asks again; parsing is by far the expensive part, so
77:// the last tree is kept. Callers must treat it as read-only.
78:let lastText: string | null = null;
79:let lastTree: Root | null = null;
80:
81:/** mdast for a document, with every read extension applied. */
82:export function parseDocument(text: string): Root {
83:  if (lastText === text && lastTree) return lastTree;
84:  // The source has to travel along: the currency guard reads it back.
85:  const tree = processor.runSync(processor.parse(text) as Root, text) as Root;
86:  lastText = text;
87:  lastTree = tree;
88:  return tree;
89:}
90:
91:/** Same headings, slugs and lines as `render()`, without building any HTML. */
92:export function headingsOf(text: string): Heading[] {
93:  return collect(parseDocument(text), new GithubSlugger(), false);
94:}

FILE src/editor/headings.ts
1:// Which heading a line sits under. The headings themselves come from the
2:// read pipeline (`headingsOf`), so both sides agree on the ids; this is only
3:// the choice, kept apart from the view so it can be tested on its own.
4:
5:import type { Heading } from "../app/store";
6:
7:/** The last heading at or above `line`, or null above the first one. */
8:export function headingAbove(headings: readonly Heading[], line: number): Heading | null {
9:  let found: Heading | null = null;
10:  for (const heading of headings) {
11:    if (heading.line > line) break;
12:    found = heading;
13:  }
14:  return found;
15:}

FILE src/read/links.ts
1:// Where a link in the rendered document points (spec §5.1, §9).
2://
3:// Paths are kept with forward slashes: Windows accepts them everywhere, and
4:// it keeps joining, comparing and `convertFileSrc` free of separator noise.
5:
6:import { slug } from "github-slugger";
7:
8:export type LinkTarget =
9:  | { kind: "external"; url: string }
10:  | { kind: "anchor"; id: string }
11:  | { kind: "file"; path: string; hash: string | null }
12:  | { kind: "none" };
13:
14:const EXTERNAL = /^(?:https?|mailto|tel):/i;
15:const HAS_EXTENSION = /\.[A-Za-z0-9]{1,8}$/;
16:
17:/** Percent-decoding that survives a stray `%` (spec: `%20`, Cyrillic). */
18:export function decode(value: string): string {
19:  try {
20:    return decodeURIComponent(value);
21:  } catch {
22:    return value;
23:  }
24:}
25:
26:export function isAbsolutePath(path: string): boolean {
27:  return /^[A-Za-z]:[\\/]/.test(path) || /^[\\/]{2}/.test(path);
28:}
29:
30:/** Joins `rel` onto `dir`, resolving `.` and `..`. */
31:export function joinPath(dir: string, rel: string): string {
32:  const parts = dir.replace(/\\/g, "/").replace(/\/+$/, "").split("/");
33:  for (const segment of rel.replace(/\\/g, "/").split("/")) {
34:    if (segment === "" || segment === ".") continue;
35:    if (segment === "..") {
36:      if (parts.length > 1) parts.pop();
37:      continue;
38:    }
39:    parts.push(segment);
40:  }
41:  return parts.join("/");
42:}
43:
44:export function normalizePath(path: string): string {
45:  return path.replace(/\\/g, "/").replace(/\/+$/, "");
46:}
47:
48:/** Case-insensitive "is this file inside that folder" (Windows). */
49:export function isInside(path: string, dir: string): boolean {
50:  const a = normalizePath(path).toLowerCase();
51:  const b = normalizePath(dir).toLowerCase();
52:  return a === b || a.startsWith(b + "/");
53:}
54:
55:function fromPath(raw: string, dir: string | null): LinkTarget {
56:  const at = raw.indexOf("#");
57:  const rawPath = at < 0 ? raw : raw.slice(0, at);
58:  const hash = at < 0 ? null : decode(raw.slice(at + 1)) || null;
59:  const path = decode(rawPath);
60:
61:  if (path === "") return hash ? { kind: "anchor", id: hash } : { kind: "none" };
62:  if (isAbsolutePath(path)) return { kind: "file", path: normalizePath(path), hash };
63:  if (!dir) return { kind: "none" };
64:  return { kind: "file", path: joinPath(dir, path), hash };
65:}
66:
67:/** `href` of an ordinary markdown link, seen from the folder of `dir`. */
68:export function resolveHref(href: string, dir: string | null): LinkTarget {
69:  const value = href.trim();
70:  if (value === "") return { kind: "none" };
71:  if (EXTERNAL.test(value)) return { kind: "external", url: value };
72:  if (value.startsWith("#")) return { kind: "anchor", id: decode(value.slice(1)) };
73:  return fromPath(value, dir);
74:}
75:
76:/** How many leading path segments two paths share. */
77:function sharedDepth(a: string, b: string): number {
78:  const left = a.toLowerCase().split("/");
79:  const right = b.toLowerCase().split("/");
80:  let depth = 0;
81:  while (depth < left.length && depth < right.length && left[depth] === right[depth]) depth += 1;
82:  return depth;
83:}
84:
85:/**
86: * A wikilink that names a note rather than a path: `[[тз]]` finds `тз.md`
87: * wherever it lives in the library, the way Obsidian resolves one. A target
88: * with folders in it (`[[projects/plan]]`) matches on that whole tail.
89: * Ties go to the file closest to the document doing the linking.
90: */
91:function byName(index: readonly string[], wanted: string, dir: string | null): string | null {
92:  const needle = normalizePath(wanted).toLowerCase();
93:  const tail = `/${needle}`;
94:  let best: string | null = null;
95:  let bestDepth = -1;
96:
97:  for (const candidate of index) {
98:    const path = normalizePath(candidate);
99:    const low = path.toLowerCase();
100:    if (low !== needle && !low.endsWith(tail)) continue;
101:    const depth = dir ? sharedDepth(path, dir) : 0;
102:    if (depth > bestDepth) {
103:      best = path;
104:      bestDepth = depth;
105:    }
106:  }
107:  return best;
108:}
109:
110:/**
111: * `[[target#Heading]]` — the target is a file name without an extension most
112: * of the time, and the heading turns into the same slug the HTML carries.
113: *
114: * `index` is the library's file list, when a library is open: a target that
115: * is not a real file next to the document is looked up by name across it.
116: */
117:export function resolveWikiLink(
118:  target: string,
119:  hash: string | null,
120:  dir: string | null,
121:  index?: readonly string[],
122:): LinkTarget {
123:  const raw = decode(target.trim());
124:  if (raw === "") return { kind: "none" };
125:  const withExtension = HAS_EXTENSION.test(raw) ? raw : `${raw}.md`;
126:  const anchor = hash ? slug(hash.trim()) : null;
127:
128:  if (isAbsolutePath(withExtension)) {
129:    return { kind: "file", path: normalizePath(withExtension), hash: anchor };
130:  }
131:
132:  const relative = dir ? joinPath(dir, withExtension) : null;
133:  if (index && index.length > 0) {
134:    // The index knows what exists, so it also says when the neighbour is not
135:    // there and the name has to be looked for somewhere else.
136:    const here = relative?.toLowerCase();
137:    const known = here !== undefined && index.some((p) => normalizePath(p).toLowerCase() === here);
138:    if (!known) {
139:      const found = byName(index, withExtension, dir);
140:      if (found) return { kind: "file", path: found, hash: anchor };
141:    }
142:  }
143:
144:  if (!relative) return { kind: "none" };
145:  return { kind: "file", path: relative, hash: anchor };
146:}
147:
148:/** Folder part of a path, with forward slashes. */
149:export function folderOf(path: string): string {
150:  const normal = normalizePath(path);
151:  const at = normal.lastIndexOf("/");
152:  return at > 0 ? normal.slice(0, at) : normal;
153:}
154:
155:/** `notes/a.md` — what the status bar shows while hovering a link. */
156:export function shortPath(path: string, dir: string | null): string {
157:  if (dir && isInside(path, dir)) {
158:    return normalizePath(path).slice(normalizePath(dir).length + 1);
159:  }
160:  return normalizePath(path);
161:}

FILE src/read/outline.ts
1:// Which heading the reader is inside right now. Read writes it while
2:// scrolling, the rail highlights it (spec §4).
3:
4:import { useSyncExternalStore } from "react";
5:
6:let active: string | null = null;
7:const listeners = new Set<() => void>();
8:
9:export function setActiveHeading(id: string | null): void {
10:  if (id === active) return;
11:  active = id;
12:  for (const listener of listeners) listener();
13:}
14:
15:function subscribe(listener: () => void): () => void {
16:  listeners.add(listener);
17:  return () => {
18:    listeners.delete(listener);
19:  };
20:}
21:
22:function snapshot(): string | null {
23:  return active;
24:}
25:
26:export function useActiveHeading(): string | null {
27:  return useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, snapshot, snapshot);
28:}
29:
30:/* ------------------------------------------------------ reading position */
31:
32:/**
33: * Which heading a document should reopen at, or null for the top.
34: *
35: * A reader sitting above the first heading is not *at* it: reopening there
36: * would scroll the meta line and the top padding off the screen, so the
37: * document would not look the way it did when it was first opened. An empty
38: * saved value means exactly that — the top — and so does the first heading,
39: * which is where the document already starts.
40: */
41:export function restoreTarget(
42:  saved: string | null,
43:  headings: readonly string[],
44:): string | null {
45:  if (!saved) return null;
46:  if (!headings.includes(saved)) return null;
47:  return saved === headings[0] ? null : saved;
48:}

FILE src/library/tree.ts
1:// The library tree (spec §6). Rust reads and sorts the folder; this side
2:// only decides what is on screen, and reloads when the watcher says the
3:// folder changed.
4:
5:import { invoke } from "@tauri-apps/api/core";
6:import { inTauri } from "../app/env";
7:import { useStore, type TreeNode } from "../app/store";
8:import { TREE_CHANGED } from "../app/watcher";
9:
10:/** A tree change is often a burst of events; one reload is enough. */
11:const RELOAD_MS = 300;
12:
13:/** One row of the rendered tree; folders carry a header, files a name. */
14:export interface Row {
15:  node: TreeNode;
16:  /** 0 at the root of the library. */
17:  depth: number;
18:  collapsed: boolean;
19:}
20:
21:/**
22: * Depth-first, skipping what is folded shut. Folders come first because Rust
23: * sorted them that way (spec §6); nothing is re-ordered here.
24: */
25:export function flatten(nodes: TreeNode[], collapsed: Set<string>, depth = 0): Row[] {
26:  const rows: Row[] = [];
27:  for (const node of nodes) {
28:    const shut = node.dir && collapsed.has(node.rel);
29:    rows.push({ node, depth, collapsed: shut });
30:    if (node.dir && !shut) rows.push(...flatten(node.children, collapsed, depth + 1));
31:  }
32:  return rows;
33:}
34:
35:/** Every file in the tree, deepest last — what quick search searches. */
36:export function files(nodes: TreeNode[]): TreeNode[] {
37:  const out: TreeNode[] = [];
38:  const walk = (list: TreeNode[]) => {
39:    for (const node of list) {
40:      if (node.dir) walk(node.children);
41:      else out.push(node);
42:    }
43:  };
44:  walk(nodes);
45:  return out;
46:}
47:
48:/** Finds a node by its absolute path; used to keep the selection alive. */
49:export function nodeAt(nodes: TreeNode[], path: string): TreeNode | null {
50:  const key = path.toLowerCase();
51:  const walk = (list: TreeNode[]): TreeNode | null => {
52:    for (const node of list) {
53:      if (node.path.toLowerCase() === key) return node;
54:      const found = node.dir ? walk(node.children) : null;
55:      if (found) return found;
56:    }
57:    return null;
58:  };
59:  return walk(nodes);
60:}
61:
62:/** Windows spelling, and it keeps whatever separator the parent used. */
63:export function joinPath(parent: string, name: string): string {
64:  const separator = parent.includes("\\") || !parent.includes("/") ? "\\" : "/";
65:  return `${parent.replace(/[\\/]+$/, "")}${separator}${name}`;
66:}
67:
68:export async function readTree(root: string, extensions: string[]): Promise<TreeNode[]> {
69:  return invoke<TreeNode[]>("read_tree", { root, extensions });
70:}
71:
72:/** `file`, `dir` or `missing` — what a dropped path turned out to be. */
73:export async function pathKind(path: string): Promise<string> {
74:  if (!inTauri) return "missing";
75:  return invoke<string>("path_kind", { path });
76:}
77:
78:/**
79: * `running` is a flag and not the promise itself on purpose: a read with no
80: * library to read finishes without ever awaiting, so the promise variable
81: * would be assigned after its own `finally` had already cleared it, and every
82: * later reload would be turned away by a read that had long since ended.
83: */
84:let running = false;
85:let pending: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
86:/** Something changed while a read was in flight; go round once more (#20). */
87:let again = false;
88:
89:/**
90: * Reads the tree again. A read that is already running is not reused — the
91: * library may have changed since it started — it is asked to repeat with
92: * whatever the parameters are by then. Failures leave the old tree alone.
93: */
94:export function reloadTree(): Promise<void> {
95:  if (running) {
96:    again = true;
97:    return pending;
98:  }
99:  running = true;
100:  pending = (async () => {
101:    try {
102:      do {
103:        again = false;
104:        const store = useStore.getState();
105:        const root = store.libraryPath;
106:        if (!root || !inTauri) {
107:          store.setTree([]);
108:          continue;
109:        }
110:        try {
111:          const tree = await readTree(root, store.settings.library.extensions);
112:          // The library may have changed under the await; if it did, the
113:          // answer is about a folder nobody is looking at any more.
114:          if (useStore.getState().libraryPath === root) useStore.getState().setTree(tree);
115:          else again = true;
116:        } catch {
117:          useStore.getState().setMessage("couldn't read the library folder");
118:        }
119:      } while (again);
120:    } finally {
121:      running = false;
122:      again = false;
123:    }
124:  })();
125:  return pending;
126:}
127:
128:/**
129: * Keeps the tree in step with the library and with the disk. The watcher is
130: * already running over the library root (spec §6); this only listens.
131: */
132:export function installTree(): () => void {
133:  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
134:  const later = () => {
135:    clearTimeout(timer);
136:    timer = setTimeout(() => void reloadTree(), RELOAD_MS);
137:  };
138:
139:  const unsubscribe = useStore.subscribe((state, previous) => {
140:    if (
141:      state.libraryPath !== previous.libraryPath ||
142:      state.settings.library.extensions !== previous.settings.library.extensions
143:    ) {
144:      void reloadTree();
145:    }
146:  });
147:
148:  window.addEventListener(TREE_CHANGED, later);
149:  void reloadTree();
150:
151:  return () => {
152:    clearTimeout(timer);
153:    unsubscribe();
154:    window.removeEventListener(TREE_CHANGED, later);
155:  };
156:}
157:
158:/**
159: * Every file in the open library, as absolute paths. A plain read of what the
160: * tree already holds — no reading of the disk, no side effects. Read uses it
161: * to resolve `[[name]]` anywhere in the library (spec §11, Obsidian's rule).
162: */
163:export function libraryFiles(): string[] {
164:  return files(useStore.getState().tree).map((node) => node.path);
165:}

FILE src/library/fuzzy.ts
1:// Quick search ranks files by their path inside the library (spec §4).
2:// uFuzzy does the matching and the ordering; this only shapes the answer so
3:// the modal can paint the matched characters with the accent.
4:
5:import uFuzzy from "@leeoniya/ufuzzy";
6:
7:export interface Hit {
8:  /** Index into the haystack that was passed in. */
9:  index: number;
10:  /** Flat `[from, to, from, to, …]` ranges over that string. */
11:  ranges: number[];
12:}
13:
14:/**
15: * One inserted character per term is enough typo room for file names.
16: *
17: * The rest is uFuzzy's own recipe for anything that is not plain latin: its
18: * defaults are written in `[A-Za-z]`, so a Cyrillic file name matched
19: * nothing at all. `\p{L}` classes need the unicode flag, which is what
20: * `unicode: true` turns on.
21: */
22:const engine = new uFuzzy({
23:  intraIns: 1,
24:  unicode: true,
25:  interSplit: "[^\\p{L}\\d']+",
26:  intraSplit: "\\p{Ll}\\p{Lu}",
27:  intraBound: "\\p{L}\\d|\\d\\p{L}|\\p{Ll}\\p{Lu}",
28:  intraChars: "[\\p{L}\\d']",
29:  intraContr: "'\\p{L}{1,2}\\b",
30:});
31:
32:/**
33: * Ranked matches, best first. An empty needle matches nothing — the modal
34: * shows `recent` instead.
35: */
36:export function rank(haystack: string[], needle: string, limit = 30): Hit[] {
37:  const query = needle.trim();
38:  if (query === "" || haystack.length === 0) return [];
39:
40:  const found = engine.filter(haystack, query);
41:  if (!found || found.length === 0) return [];
42:
43:  const info = engine.info(found, haystack, query);
44:  const order = engine.sort(info, haystack, query);
45:
46:  const hits: Hit[] = [];
47:  for (const position of order) {
48:    const index = info.idx[position];
49:    if (index === undefined) continue;
50:    hits.push({ index, ranges: info.ranges[position] ?? [] });
51:    if (hits.length >= limit) break;
52:  }
53:  return hits;
54:}
55:
56:export interface Piece {
57:  text: string;
58:  hit: boolean;
59:}
60:
61:/** Splits a string into plain and matched pieces, ready to render. */
62:export function pieces(text: string, ranges: number[]): Piece[] {
63:  const out: Piece[] = [];
64:  let at = 0;
65:  for (let i = 0; i + 1 < ranges.length; i += 2) {
66:    const from = ranges[i] as number;
67:    const to = ranges[i + 1] as number;
68:    if (from > at) out.push({ text: text.slice(at, from), hit: false });
69:    out.push({ text: text.slice(from, to), hit: true });
70:    at = to;
71:  }
72:  if (at < text.length) out.push({ text: text.slice(at), hit: false });
73:  return out;
74:}

291:
292:  // Same as applySettings, plus the write. Startup uses the other one: it
293:  // would otherwise write the file back the moment it read it.
294:  changeSettings: (settings) => {
295:    get().applySettings(settings);
296:    saveSettings(settings);
297:  },
298:
299:  setSettingsOpen: (settingsOpen) => set({ settingsOpen }),
300:  setShortcutsOpen: (shortcutsOpen) => set({ shortcutsOpen }),
301:  setZoom: (zoom) => set({ zoom }),
302:
303:  // The theme is a setting like any other, so picking one writes the file
304:  // (spec §10) — from the status bar, Ctrl+Shift+D or the view menu.
305:  setTheme: (theme) => {
306:    const settings = { ...get().settings, appearance: { ...get().settings.appearance, theme } };
307:    applyTheme(theme);
308:    set({ settings, theme, resolvedTheme: resolveTheme(theme) });
309:    saveSettings(settings);
310:  },
311:
312:  /** Toggling out of "system" lands on the opposite of what is on screen. */
313:  toggleTheme: () => get().setTheme(flipTheme(get().resolvedTheme)),
314:
315:  syncSystemTheme: (resolved) => {
316:    if (get().theme === "system") set({ resolvedTheme: resolved });
317:  },
318:
319:  toggleRail: () => set((s) => ({ railCollapsed: !s.railCollapsed })),
320:  setRailCollapsed: (railCollapsed) => set({ railCollapsed }),
321:  setRailView: (railView) => set({ railView }),
322:  setLibraryPath: (libraryPath) => set({ libraryPath }),
323:
324:  openDoc: (doc) => {
325:    if (get().docs.some((d) => d.id === doc.id)) {
326:      set({ activeId: doc.id });
327:      return;
328:    }
329:    const path = doc.path;
330:    set((s) => ({
331:      docs: [...s.docs, doc],
332:      activeId: doc.id,
333:      recent: path ? [path, ...s.recent.filter((p) => p !== path)].slice(0, 20) : s.recent,
334:    }));
335:  },
336:
337:  updateDoc: (id, patch) =>
338:    set((s) => ({
339:      docs: s.docs.map((d) => {
340:        if (d.id !== id) return d;
341:        const next = { ...d, ...patch };
342:        // Every real change to the text moves the revision on; §8 leans on it.
343:        if (patch.text !== undefined && patch.text !== d.text) {
344:          next.revision = d.revision + 1;
345:        }
346:        return next;
347:      }),
348:    })),
349:
350:  renameDoc: (id, path) => {
351:    const next = pathKey(path);
352:    set((s) => ({
353:      docs: s.docs.map((d) =>
354:        d.id === id ? { ...d, id: next, path, title: basename(path) } : d,
355:      ),
356:      activeId: s.activeId === id ? next : s.activeId,
357:      recent: [path, ...s.recent.filter((p) => p !== path)].slice(0, 20),
358:    }));
359:    return next;
360:  },
361:
362:  activate: (id) => set({ activeId: id }),
363:
364:  closeDoc: (id) =>
365:    set((s) => {
366:      const index = s.docs.findIndex((d) => d.id === id);
367:      if (index < 0) return {};
368:      const docs = s.docs.filter((d) => d.id !== id);
369:      if (s.activeId !== id) return { docs };
370:      const next = docs[Math.min(index, docs.length - 1)];
371:      return { docs, activeId: next ? next.id : null };
372:    }),
373:
374:  cycleDoc: (step) => {
375:    const { docs, activeId } = get();
376:    if (docs.length < 2) return;
377:    const index = docs.findIndex((d) => d.id === activeId);
378:    const next = docs[(index + step + docs.length) % docs.length];
379:    if (next) set({ activeId: next.id });
380:  },
381:
382:  setMode: (mode) =>
383:    set((s) => ({
384:      docs: s.docs.map((d) => (d.id === s.activeId ? { ...d, mode } : d)),
385:    })),
386:
387:  // `Ctrl+/` is the read/edit switch (spec §4); from rich it goes to read.
388:  toggleMode: () =>
389:    set((s) => ({
390:      docs: s.docs.map((d) =>
391:        d.id === s.activeId ? { ...d, mode: d.mode === "read" ? "edit" : "read" } : d,
392:      ),
393:    })),
394:
395:  setMessage: (message) => {
396:    clearTimeout(messageTimer);
397:    set({ message });
398:    if (message !== null) {
399:      messageTimer = setTimeout(() => set({ message: null }), 3000);
400:    }
401:  },
402:
403:  setNote: (note) => {
404:    clearTimeout(noteTimer);
405:    set({ note });
406:    if (note !== null) {
407:      noteTimer = setTimeout(() => set({ note: null }), 3000);
408:    }
409:  },
410:
411:  // Only one banner at a time (spec §4), so the more urgent one wins.
412:  showBanner: (banner) =>
413:    set((s) => (s.banner && rank(s.banner.id) > rank(banner.id) ? {} : { banner })),
414:  dismissBanner: (id) => set((s) => (id && s.banner?.id !== id ? {} : { banner: null })),
415:  setAlwaysOnTop: (alwaysOnTop) => set({ alwaysOnTop }),
416:  setDialog: (dialog) => set({ dialog }),
417:  setRecovery: (recovery) => set({ recovery }),
418:  setRecent: (recent) => set({ recent }),
419:  forgetRecent: (path) =>
420:    set((s) => {
421:      const key = pathKey(path);
422:      const recent = s.recent.filter((p) => pathKey(p) !== key);
423:      // The session only writes when something changed, so keep identity.
424:      return recent.length === s.recent.length ? {} : { recent };
425:    }),
426:
427:  setTree: (tree) => set({ tree, treeFiles: countFiles(tree) }),
428:  setCollapsed: (collapsed) => set({ collapsed }),
429:  toggleCollapsed: (rel) =>
430:    set((s) => ({
431:      collapsed: s.collapsed.includes(rel)
432:        ? s.collapsed.filter((r) => r !== rel)
433:        : [...s.collapsed, rel],
434:    })),
435:  setTreeSelected: (treeSelected) => set({ treeSelected }),
436:  setTreeDraft: (treeDraft) => set({ treeDraft }),
437:  setTreeRenaming: (treeRenaming) => set({ treeRenaming }),
438:  setQuickSearch: (quickSearch) => set({ quickSearch }),
439:  setFolderSearch: (folderSearch) => set({ folderSearch }),
440:}));
441:
442:export function activeDoc(state: AppState): Doc | null {
443:  return state.docs.find((d) => d.id === state.activeId) ?? null;
444:}

FILE src/app/commands.ts
444:/** Position just past the matching close tag of an element already opened. */
445:function skipElement(html: string, from: number, name: string): number {
446:  let depth = 1;
447:  let i = from;
448:  const tags = new RegExp(`<(/?)${name}\\b[^>]*>`, "gi");
449:  tags.lastIndex = from;
450:  for (let m = tags.exec(html); m; m = tags.exec(html)) {
451:    depth += m[1] ? -1 : 1;
452:    i = tags.lastIndex;
453:    if (depth === 0) return i;
454:  }
455:  return html.length;
456:}
457:
458:/**
459: * The document as it looks, not as it is written. Null when it is too big to
460: * render at all (spec §8).
461: */
462:export function plainTextOf(doc: Doc): string | null {
463:  if (doc.large) return null;
464:  return htmlToPlainText(render(doc.text).html);
465:}
466:
467:/** `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C`: the document as plain text, on the clipboard. */
468:export async function copyPlainText(): Promise<void> {
469:  const store = useStore.getState();
470:  flushActiveEditor();
471:  const doc = activeDoc(useStore.getState());
472:  if (!doc) return;
473:
474:  const text = plainTextOf(doc);
475:  if (text === null) {
476:    store.setMessage("too large to render");
477:    return;
478:  }
479:  try {
480:    await navigator.clipboard.writeText(text);
481:    store.setMessage(`copied as plain text · ${countWords(text).toLocaleString("en-US")} words`);
482:  } catch {
483:    store.setMessage("couldn't copy");
484:  }
485:}
486:
487:/** `file → export as text…`: the same text, written as a `.txt`. */
488:export async function exportPlainText(): Promise<void> {
489:  if (!inTauri) return;
490:  const store = useStore.getState();
491:  flushActiveEditor();
492:  const doc = activeDoc(useStore.getState());
493:  if (!doc) return;
494:
495:  const text = plainTextOf(doc);
496:  if (text === null) {
497:    store.setMessage("too large to render");
498:    return;
499:  }
500:
501:  const name = doc.path ? basename(doc.path) : doc.title;
502:  const picked = await save({
503:    defaultPath: `${name.replace(/\.[^.]+$/, "")}.txt`,
504:    filters: [{ name: "text", extensions: ["txt"] }],
505:  });
506:  if (typeof picked !== "string") return;
507:
508:  try {
509:    // UTF-8, no BOM; the line endings new files get (spec §10).
510:    await writeTextAtomic(picked, serialize(text, store.settings.files.newFileEol));
511:    store.setMessage(`exported ${basename(picked)}`);
512:  } catch (error) {
513:    store.setMessage(`couldn't export — ${fsError(error).message}`);
514:  }
515:}
516:
517:/* ------------------------------------------------------- pdf (spec §4) */
518:
519:/**
520: * `Ctrl+P`. Printing prints what is on screen, so the document has to be in
521: * read first; the mode goes back after the dialog closes.
522: */
523:export async function exportPdf(): Promise<void> {
524:  const store = useStore.getState();
525:  const doc = activeDoc(store);
526:  if (!doc) return;
527:
528:  const was = doc.mode;
529:  if (was !== "read") {
530:    flushActiveEditor();
531:    store.setMode("read");
532:    // The read view builds the document on entry; printing before it is
533:    // ready would print an empty page.
534:    await new Promise((done) => setTimeout(done, 400));
535:  }
536:
537:  const restore = () => {
538:    window.removeEventListener("afterprint", restore);
539:    if (was !== "read") useStore.getState().setMode(was);
540:  };
541:  window.addEventListener("afterprint", restore);
542:
543:  try {
544:    window.print();
545:  } catch {
546:    store.setMessage("couldn't open the print dialog");
547:    restore();
548:  }
549:}

1:import { useEffect, useLayoutEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
2:import { EditorView, type Command as EditorCommand } from "@codemirror/view";
3:import type { Doc } from "../app/store";
4:import { useStore } from "../app/store";
5:import { buffer, keepBuffer, markSaved, moveBuffer, setBufferText } from "./buffers";
6:import { headingsOf } from "../read/headings";
7:import { headingAbove } from "./headings";
8:import { richConf, richExtension } from "./rich";
9:import { gutterConf, gutterExtension, lineNumbersOn, onLineNumbers } from "./setup";
10:import { flushText, setSyncTarget, syncCaret } from "./sync";
11:import "../ui/editor.css";

13:const GOTO_LINE = "plain:goto-line";
14:const GOTO_HEADING = "plain:goto-heading";
15:/** A jump sent just before this view mounts is still ours; an older one is not. */
16:const PENDING_MS = 2000;
17:
18:let live: EditorView | null = null;
19:let liveId: string | null = null;
20:let pending: { goto: Goto; at: number } | null = null;
21:
22:/**
23: * Puts whatever the editor holds into the store synchronously. Wave 4 calls
24: * this before saving, so the snapshot never waits for the idle callback.
25: */
26:export function flushActiveEditor(): void {
27:  if (live && liveId) flushText(live, liveId);
28:}
29:
30:/**
31: * Runs a CodeMirror command against whichever editor is on screen. The menu
32: * bar needs this: undo, bold and friends are editor commands, and the menu
33: * has no view of its own (spec §4). Nothing mounted — nothing happens.
34: */
35:export function runEditorCommand(command: EditorCommand): boolean {
36:  if (!live) return false;
37:  // The menu took the focus away; the command works on the selection anyway,
38:  // but the caret has to come back or the next keystroke goes nowhere.
39:  live.focus();
40:  return command(live);
41:}
42:
43:/**
44: * Replaces the whole document in one transaction, so a silent reload from
45: * disk is a single undo step (spec §8). The new text becomes the clean one.
46: */
47:export function replaceText(id: string, text: string): void {
48:  if (live && liveId === id) {
49:    live.dispatch({ changes: { from: 0, to: live.state.doc.length, insert: text } });
50:    keepBuffer(id, live.state, live.scrollDOM.scrollTop);
51:    markSaved(id, text);
52:    // The dispatch queued a sync that would report the document as edited;
53:    // running it now, after the new text is the clean one, settles it.
54:    flushText(live, id);
55:    return;
56:  }
57:  setBufferText(id, text);
58:  markSaved(id, text);
59:}
60:
61:/**
62: * Save As: the buffer follows the document to its new id. Whatever the live
63: * editor holds is stored first, or the move would carry a stale state.
64: */
65:export function renameBuffer(from: string, to: string): void {
66:  if (live && liveId === from) {
67:    keepBuffer(from, live.state, live.scrollDOM.scrollTop);
68:    liveId = to;
69:  }
70:  moveBuffer(from, to);
71:}
72:
73:/**
74: * Where a `plain:goto-line` wants the editor. `moveCaret` is explicit when
75: * the sender says so (the outline moves the caret, a mode switch only
76: * scrolls); a bare line number means "move it unless that would throw away a
77: * selection" (review #7).
78: */
79:interface Goto {
80:  line: number;
81:  moveCaret: boolean | "auto";
82:}
83:
84:function readGoto(detail: unknown): Goto | null {
85:  if (typeof detail === "number") return { line: detail, moveCaret: "auto" };
86:  if (detail && typeof detail === "object") {
87:    const it = detail as { line?: unknown; moveCaret?: unknown };
88:    if (typeof it.line === "number") {
89:      return { line: it.line, moveCaret: it.moveCaret !== false };
90:    }
91:  }
92:  return null;
93:}
94:
95:function jump(view: EditorView, goto: Goto): void {
96:  const target = view.state.doc.line(Math.min(Math.max(1, goto.line), view.state.doc.lines));
97:  const move =
98:    goto.moveCaret === "auto" ? view.state.selection.main.empty : goto.moveCaret;
99:  // Focus first: focusing after the transaction makes the browser scroll to
100:  // where the selection used to be, undoing the jump.
101:  view.focus();
102:  view.dispatch({
103:    ...(move ? { selection: { anchor: target.from } } : {}),
104:    effects: EditorView.scrollIntoView(target.from, { y: "start" }),
105:  });
106:}
107:
108:// The outline and read -> edit send this; it can arrive before the view is
109:// mounted, so it waits here until it is. The listener lives on the module so
110:// it is there before the first mount; the window slot keeps a hot reload from
111:// leaving the previous module's listener (and its dead view) behind.
112:const LISTENER_SLOT = "__plainGotoLine";
113:
114:if (typeof window !== "undefined") {
115:  const slot = window as unknown as Record<string, EventListener | undefined>;

FILE src/ui/DocView.tsx
1:import type { Doc } from "../app/store";
2:import { ReadView } from "../read/ReadView";
3:import { EditView } from "../editor/EditView";
4:
5:/**
6: * Three views over one buffer (spec §5.0): read renders it, edit shows the
7: * source, rich is edit with the markers hidden (§5.3).
8: */
9:export function DocView({ doc }: { doc: Doc }) {
10:  if (doc.mode === "read") return <ReadView doc={doc} />;
11:  return <EditView doc={doc} rich={doc.mode === "rich"} />;
12:}

FILE src/ui/Shortcuts.tsx
1:// The key list (spec §12), `F1` and `help → shortcuts`. It used to live in
2:// the README; it belongs where the keys are. Everything with a chord comes
3:// out of the command registry, so this page cannot drift from the bindings.
4:
5:import { Fragment, useEffect } from "react";
6:import { chordText } from "../app/menu";
7:import { commands } from "../app/registry";
8:import { useStore } from "../app/store";
9:import "./dialogs.css";
10:import "./settings.css";
11:
12:type Group =
13:  | "files"
14:  | "navigation"
15:  | "view"
16:  | "formatting"
17:  | "editing"
18:  | "tree"
19:  | "other";
20:
21:const ORDER: Group[] = [
22:  "files",
23:  "navigation",
24:  "view",
25:  "formatting",
26:  "editing",
27:  "tree",
28:  "other",
29:];
30:
31:/** Command id prefix -> the group it is listed under. */
32:const BY_PREFIX: { prefix: string; group: Group }[] = [
33:  { prefix: "file.", group: "files" },
34:  { prefix: "nav.", group: "navigation" },
35:  { prefix: "view.", group: "view" },
36:  { prefix: "edit.", group: "formatting" },
37:  { prefix: "app.", group: "other" },
38:];
39:
40:/** Undo and redo are `edit.` ids but they are not formatting. */
41:const MOVED: Record<string, Group> = { "edit.undo": "editing", "edit.redo": "editing" };
42:
43:/** `Ctrl+1…6` is one line in the spec; six rows would only be noise. */
44:const HEADINGS = "edit.heading1";
45:const SKIP = new Set(["edit.heading2", "edit.heading3", "edit.heading4", "edit.heading5", "edit.heading6"]);
46:
47:interface Row {
48:  key: string;
49:  label: string;
50:  keys: string;
51:}
52:
53:/**
54: * Bindings that belong to CodeMirror's own keymap (@codemirror/commands and
55: * @codemirror/search), not to our registry — they are standard CM6 and are
56: * listed here by hand so the page is the whole truth (spec §5.2).
57: */
58:const STANDARD: Record<string, Row[]> = {
59:  editing: [
60:    { key: "cm-multi", label: "select next occurrence", keys: "ctrl d" },
61:    { key: "cm-cursor", label: "add cursor above / below", keys: "ctrl alt ↑ ↓" },
62:    { key: "cm-move", label: "move line", keys: "alt ↑ ↓" },
63:    { key: "cm-dup", label: "duplicate line", keys: "alt shift ↑ ↓" },
64:    { key: "cm-del", label: "delete line", keys: "ctrl shift x" },
65:    { key: "cm-fold", label: "fold / unfold", keys: "ctrl shift [ ]" },
66:    { key: "cm-plain", label: "paste as plain text", keys: "ctrl shift v" },
67:    { key: "cm-tab", label: "indent / outdent list item", keys: "tab · shift tab" },
68:  ],
69:  tree: [
70:    { key: "tree-open", label: "open", keys: "enter" },
71:    { key: "tree-move", label: "move", keys: "↑ ↓" },
72:    { key: "tree-rename", label: "rename", keys: "f2" },
73:    { key: "tree-delete", label: "delete to recycle bin", keys: "delete" },
74:  ],
75:};
76:
77:function groupOf(id: string): Group {
78:  const moved = MOVED[id];
79:  if (moved) return moved;
80:  return BY_PREFIX.find((entry) => id.startsWith(entry.prefix))?.group ?? "other";
81:}
82:
83:function rows(): Record<Group, Row[]> {
84:  const out = Object.fromEntries(ORDER.map((g) => [g, [] as Row[]])) as Record<Group, Row[]>;
85:
86:  for (const command of commands) {
87:    // `hint` is a chord the editor's keymap or WebView2 owns; it is still a
88:    // key the reader can press, so it belongs on this page.
89:    const chord = command.chord ?? command.hint;
90:    if (!chord || SKIP.has(command.id)) continue;
91:    const heading = command.id === HEADINGS;
92:    out[groupOf(command.id)].push({
93:      key: command.id,
94:      label: heading ? "heading 1–6" : command.title,
95:      keys: heading ? "ctrl 1 … ctrl 6" : chordText(chord),
96:    });
97:  }
98:
99:  for (const [group, list] of Object.entries(STANDARD)) {
100:    out[group as Group].push(...list);
101:  }
102:  return out;
103:}
104:
105:export function ShortcutsScreen() {
106:  const close = () => useStore.getState().setShortcutsOpen(false);
107:
108:  useEffect(() => {
109:    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
110:      if (event.key !== "Escape") return;
111:      if (useStore.getState().dialog) return;
112:      event.preventDefault();
113:      event.stopPropagation();
114:      close();
115:    };
116:    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
117:    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey, { capture: true });
118:  }, []);
119:
120:  const grouped = rows();
121:
122:  return (
123:    <div className="screen">
124:      <div className="screen-column settings-column">
125:        <div className="settings-head">
126:          <h1 className="screen-title">shortcuts</h1>
127:          <button className="link" onClick={close}>
128:            close
129:          </button>
130:        </div>
131:
132:        {ORDER.map((group) => {
133:          const list = grouped[group];
134:          if (list.length === 0) return null;
135:          return (
136:            <Fragment key={group}>
137:              <div className="keys-group section-title">{group}</div>
138:              {list.map((row) => (
139:                <div className="keys-row" key={row.key}>
140:                  <span className="keys-what">{row.label}</span>
141:                  <span className="keys-chord">{row.keys}</span>
142:                </div>
143:              ))}
144:            </Fragment>
145:          );
146:        })}
147:
148:        <div className="keys-note">shortcuts work in any keyboard layout</div>
149:      </div>
150:    </div>
151:  );
152:}
